The X switch tells Windows installer to remove the application and the value inside the curly braces is the product code also known as GUID,G-U-I-D.

This uniquely identifies the install application in Windows and allows Intune to remove it reliably.

And the QN parameter again ensures the uninstall runs silently without prompting the user.

Using the product GUID is the most reliable method for uninstalling MSI applications in Intune.

Next,we will set the installation time required for 60 minutes.

This gives Intune sufficient time to complete the deployment before marking it as failed,which is especially useful in lab or low performance environments.

Next,we will leave allow available uninstall set to yes,which allows user to remove the application from the company portal if needed.

And we will leave the install behavior set to system.

This ensures the application install at the device level regardless of which user is signed in.

This is the recommended and most common option for corporate Win32 application.

Next,we will keep the default restart behavior to app install may force a device restart.

If the application requires a restart,Intune will handle it automatically.

At the end of the program settings,we have written code.

These return codes tell Intune how to interpret the installer response.

Code like 01707 indicates success while 3010 and 1641 indicates that a reboot is required.

The default values are already correct,so no changes are needed here.

Click Next.

Now we will configure the requirements which define which devices are eligible to receive this Win32 application.

For the first option,check operating system architecture.

We'll choose No.Allow this app to be installed on all systems.

So we are not restricting the app by architecture in this lab.

Next,we have minimum operating system.

This field is mandatory for Win32 applications.

We will select Windows 10 version 1607,which is the lowest supported baseline.

This ensures the app can install on all supported Windows 10 versions and all Windows 11 devices.

And for this lab,we will leave all the other fields empty and click Next.

Now we will configure detection rules,which tell in tune how to verify whether the application is already installed on the device.

This step is extremely important.

If Intune cannot detect the app correctly,it may keep reinstalling it or report the deployment incorrectly.

So for the rules format,we will select manually configure detect rules.

This option allows us to define exactly how Intune checks for the presence of the application,which is the recommended approach for MSI-based PIN32 applications.

For MSI applications like 7-Zip,the most reliable detection method is checking the MSI product code.

Intune can directly query Windows installer to confirm whether the app is installed.

We'll go ahead and click on Add.

To add a rule,we'll choose the rule type MSI and we'll leave the MSI product code as it is and we'll leave the MSI product version check set to no.Here we are using the same product code GUID that we used in the uninstall command.

If Windows reports this product code as installed,Intune considers the app successfully deployed.

Once done,click on OK and click Next.

Now we are at the dependencies step.

Dependencies are applications that must be installed before this app can be installed.

In this case,7-zip does not require any prerequisite application,so we don't need to add anything here.

If this were an app that depended on another component,for example,Visual C,Redistributables,or.NET,we would add them here and choose whether they should be installed automatically.

Click Next.

Under the Supersedence tab,this section allows us to specify which existing apps this new app should replace.

For example,if we were deploying a new version of 7-zip,we could configure it here automatically,replace the older version.

For this lab,we are deploying 7-zip for the first time,so there is no older version to upgrade or replace.

Because of that,we will leave supersedence empty.

Click Next.

Now we will configure assignments which determine who actually received this application.

Until an app is assigned,Intune will not deploy it to any device.

In the required section,applications are installed automatically on target devices without user interaction.

For this lab,we will assign this app as required so that it installs silently in the background.

You can use the group for the devices here if you have one.

If you don't have a group yet,you can use add all devices in a lab environment.

But in production,we always recommend using scoped groups.

Next section is available for enrolled devices.

This section makes the app available in the company portal,allowing users to install it themselves.

For this lab,we will leave this section empty because we want to demonstrate an automatic deployment,not self-service.

And the uninstall assignment is used when you want Intune to remove an app from devices.

For this lab,we are not testing app removal,so we'll leave this section empty.

For the required section,we will leave all notification,availability and delivery optimization setting at their defaults.

These options are typically customized in production but are not required for this lab.

You probably have noticed on the top there is a blue banner.

This notice reminds us that Win32 apps are not automatically removed when a device is retired.

If an app must be removed,it should be explicitly uninstalled before retiring the device.

Once done,click on Next.

Review your settings.

Once reviewed,click on Create.

As you can see,the application saved successfully.

We have created the Win32 app,assigned it,and Intune is now responsible for getting it onto the endpoint.

Now we do the most important part of the deployment,which is validation.

Because the portal can look perfect while the device still has not received the policy yet.

Portals show intent,endpoints show reality.

First,we will go back to our Windows endpoints,Windows 10.

On the endpoint,I'm going to trigger a sync so the device immediately checks in the Intune instead of waiting for the normal schedule.

Go into the settings,Accounts,Access Worker School,Manage User,click Info and click on Sync.

We'll switch to our Windows 11 and do the same.

Settings and click on Sync.

Now we wait a moment.

In most labs,you will need about 1 to 2 minutes depending on the VM performance and the network.

While we wait,remember what we are expecting,Intune will download the Win32 content,run the install command silently at system and run the detection rule to confirm the app is installed.

The detection rule is what flips the deployment from in progress to succeeded.

As you can see,we have received a notification on Windows 10 that Microsoft Intune software distribution downloading and installing software 7zip Win32.

As you can see,Microsoft Intune software distribution notification and it says 7-zip Win32 was installed successfully.

We'll switch to Windows 11,click on start and type 7-zip.

So we cannot see 7-zip as of now,we need to wait.

Now as we can see we have also received the same thing on Windows 11 virtual machine that it is downloading and installing software 7-zip Win32 and the 7-zip Win32 was installed successfully.

Perfect.

Now we will validate the same result in the Intune Admin Center.

We will navigate to our Intune Admin Center,click on Apps,click on Windows and we can confirm that 7-zip Win32 is there.

Now click on 7-zip app and we can see that the device status shows installed on two endpoints.

If you see pending or installing,don't panic.

That usually just mean the device has not completed its check-in cycle yet.

Trigger one more sync,wait another minute,and refresh the status page.

Once the endpoint shows 7-zip installed and Intune shows succeeded,we are done.

That means portal reporting an endpoint reality match.

And that's what a successful Win32 deployment looks like.

You just completed a real enterprise Win32 deployment.

You packaged correctly,you defined detection logic you assigned intentionally,and you validate it like a professional,not by guessing.

This is the foundation of Intune application management.

In the next lab,we will break this deployment on purpose and troubleshoot it.

Because knowing how things fail is what separates admin from engineers.

73. 实验7-2：破坏WIN32应用并进行故障排除

欢迎进入实验七点二。

破坏一个Win32应用程序并进行故障排除。

在我们动手操作任何东西之前，让我们重置一下预期，因为这个实验关乎思维模式，而不是点击按钮。

在Intune中，部署的成功与否并不取决于应用程序是否能打开或运行。

Intune仅基于一件事来判断成功，那就是检测逻辑。

因此，即使应用程序实际上已安装在设备上并正常工作，如果检测规则无法证明这一点，Intune仍可能将其报告为失败。

对于来自SCCM或手动安装的管理员来说，这是一个巨大的思维转变，因为他们通常相信桌面上看到的情况。

现在来看看为什么这在现实世界中如此重要。

许多生产环境中的事件都以同样的方式开始。

用户说应用程序在那里，但门户显示失败。

如果你不理解检测机制，你就会浪费数小时去重新安装或重新打包一些原本就没有损坏的东西。

所以在这个实验中，我们将故意创建完全相同的场景。

我们将安全地故意破坏检测功能，以便我们能够学会正确地对其进行故障排除。

以下是我们将遵循的流程，我希望你在进行过程中将这个结构牢记在心。

首先，我们破坏检测。

其次，我们观察Intune在门户中报告的内容。

第三，我们将该报告与终端实际情况进行比较，意思是应用程序实际上是否已安装。

然后，我们利用包括日志在内的证据来证明根本原因，而不是靠猜测。

最后，我们通过恢复正确的检测并确认状态返回成功，来正确地解决问题。

还有一件重要的事，不要急于完成这个实验。

这正是真实事件在生产环境中展开的方式。

你会看到相互矛盾的信号，你会看到报告的延迟，你也会忍不住想要重新部署。

但这里的全部意义在于慢下来，像工程师一样进行故障排除。

Intune是怎么想的？

它为什么这么想？

终端证明了什么？

如果你掌握了这项技能，Win32故障排除将变得可预测，而不再充满压力。

好了，确立了这种思维模式后，让我们从破坏检测开始。

首先，我将打开Intune管理中心，转到“应用”，然后选择“Windows”，并打开我们在实验七点一中现有的七Zip Win32应用程序。

现在我们在页面上，我将进入“属性”，然后滚动到“检测规则”。

点击它。

这里是关键细节。

我们的检测方法是MSI。

这意味着Intune使用MSI产品代码检查Windows安装程序数据库。

请注意一个重要的地方，当我点击检测规则上的三个点时，我没有看到“编辑”选项。

我只看到“删除”。

这不是错误。

对于Intune中的MSI检测规则来说，这是正常的。

如果你需要更改MSI产品代码，你必须删除该规则并创建一个新规则。

所以现在我们要破坏它。

我将点击三个点并选择“删除”。

你还会看到一个红色横幅，提示需要检测规则。

这意味着我们没有任何检测规则到位。

此时，Intune无法确认七Zip是否已安装。

即使应用程序物理上存在于设备上，Intune也无法证明它。

现在让我们添加一个虚假的检测规则。

点击“添加”。

这次，规则类型应为“文件”。

请注意，我们故意将检测指向一个不存在的路径。因此，即使应用程序实际上已安装，Intune的检测也会失败。

在文件或文件夹中，我将指定七z点exe。

我将“关联六十位客户端上的三十二位应用程序”保留为“否”，然后点击“确定”。

完成后，点击“查看加保存”，然后保存。

在下一步中，我们将重新同步终端，刷新门户，并观察失败状态的出现。

然后我们将正确地进行故障排除。

让我们切换回我们的Windows终端。

现在，这一步无意中被设计得很枯燥，而这正是它重要的原因。

这是大多数管理员感到不舒服的地方，因为似乎什么都没发生。

但这段等待时间并非wasted time。

这才是Intune真正的工作方式。

Intune不会立即评估应用程序。

幕后并没有实时的“安装失败”按钮。

设备按计划进行检查，报告状态，然后Intune基于这些数据评估检测情况。

这种延迟是三十二位故障排除在真实环境中让人感到困惑的主要原因之一。

你在门户中更改了某些内容，然后盯着屏幕wondering它是否生效。

如果你不理解这种时间模型，当实际上只是尚未评估时，你会认为出了问题。

现在，请记住我们在上一步做了什么，我们没有破坏安装程序，没有卸载应用程序，我们只破坏了检测。

所以Intune即将做的是重新评估设备并提出一个简单的问题：我还能检测到这个应用程序吗？

由于检测已被破坏，Intune将得出结论认为应用程序缺失，即使它实际上仍安装在设备上。

这就是门户现实与终端现实开始分离的时刻。

而这个差距正是大多数故障排除错误发生的地方。

所以不要着急，不要重新部署，不要重新安装，只需观察。

在Windows十上，我们点击“同步”，在我们的Windows十一上也做同样的操作。

同步。

一旦触发同步，就不要做其他任何事。

不要重启。

不要打开应用程序，只需等待。

至少等待两到三分钟。

两到三分钟后，让我们导航回Intune管理中心。

点击“应用”，点击“Windows”，然后点击“七Zip Win32”。

现在在这里暂停一下，仔细看看Intune在告诉你什么。

注意状态已变为“失败”。

请记住，我们根本没有触碰应用程序本身。

这是关键的一课。

Intune并不是说安装失败了。

Intune是在说我无法证明应用程序已安装。

这种区别在Win32故障排除中比几乎任何其他事情都重要。

在妄下结论之前，我们总是先验证终端。

Intune只告诉我们检测逻辑的想法。

设备告诉我们实际发生了什么。

这一步至关重要，因为许多现实世界中的升级都始于“失败意味着损坏”的假设。

而这往往是错误的。

我们将切换回我们的Windows终端，在我们的Windows十虚拟机上。

转到“开始”并搜索“七Zip”。

启动七Zip文件管理器并确认应用程序正常打开。

这是关键的一课。

应用程序已安装且工作正常。

只有检测被破坏了。

Intune并没有让安装失败。

它让识别成功失败。

这种确切的不匹配是Intune支持工单最常见的原因之一。

现在我们停止猜测，我们来证明发生了什么。

Intune门户报告结果，如已安装、失败或重试，但并不总是告诉你原因。

日志才是真相所在。

如果你不阅读日志，你最终会重新安装、重新打包或责怪设备，而真正的问题往往很简单，比如检测逻辑。

在我们的案例中，我们预计安装没问题，因为应用程序可以打开。

所以我们的目标是找到证据证明Intune在检测步骤失败，而不是在安装步骤失败。

此时，我们停止收集数据，因为我们已经有了足够的信息。

让我们总结一下我们所知道的。

Intune显示失败。

应用程序成功打开。

没有安装错误。

日志没有明确指出安装失败。

所以，与其追逐那些在这里帮不上忙的日志，不如应用逻辑。

Intune只有三个阶段。

下载、安装、检测。

下载发生了。

安装发生了。

所以如果Intune仍然说失败，那么只能是检测负责。

让我们导航回Intune管理中心。

并在检测规则下观察，它指向一个特定的路径。

该路径被故意设置为一个不存在的位置。

大声问出这个问题。

如果这个路径不存在，检测能返回真吗？

现在我们修复唯一被破坏的东西，那就是检测逻辑。

重要的现实检查：Intune不会立即恢复。

它不会立即将失败翻转为成功。

它也不会仅仅因为检测被修复了就重新安装应用程序。

我们在这里所做的是恢复Intune所依赖的真实信号。

我们在告诉Intune：这是你正确确认应用程序存在的方式。

一旦检测被纠正，应用程序不会重新安装。

Intune会在下次检查期间重新评估检测，只有那时才会更新状态。

这种延迟是正常的。

这就是为什么管理员认为Intune坏了，而实际上它只是异步的。

所以在检测规则下，我们将删除我们的虚假路径。

然后再次点击“添加”。

运行类型选MSI，我们可以看到MSI代码已自动检测到原始的七Zip产品代码。

我们将“检查MSI产品版本”设置为“否”，然后点击“确定”。

然后我们将查看并保存，再保存。

现在让我们从我们的两个Windows终端触发同步。

Windows十同步和Windows十一。

点击同步。

现在让我们等待三到五分钟。

不要重新安装，不要反复强制操作。

现在三到五分钟后，回到“应用”、“Windows”并点击七Zip应用程序。

我们可以看到应用程序状态从“失败”变为“已安装”。

这是Win32应用程序故障排除中最重要的一课。

修复检测并不能修复过去。它修复的是下一次评估。

如果你期望立即恢复，你会认为Intune不可靠。

如果你不理解检测周期，Intune就会变得不可预测。

这个实验代表了真实的Intune工程实践。

你学到了为什么应用程序即使已安装也会失败，为什么检测规则定义了真相，为什么终端验证很重要，为什么日志比仪表板更重要，如何在不干扰用户的情况下解决问题，为什么日志比仪表板更重要，以及如何在不干扰用户的情况下解决问题。

这就是只会点击按钮的人与理解系统的人之间的区别。

有了这种规模，Win32故障排除就不再可怕了。

让我们在这里暂停一下，反思一下你实际上学到了什么，因为这个实验比它看起来更重要。

这不仅仅是关于部署七Zip。

这个实验向你展示了Intune真正的思考方式。

首先，你学到了一些关键的东西。

应用程序可能已安装，但仍被报告为失败。

起初这感觉不对，但现在你明白了为什么。

Intune不检查应用程序是否打开。

它不在乎用户是否满意。

它只问一个问题。

检测规则返回真了吗？

这是第一个重大的思维转变。

其次，你学到了为什么Win32故障排除让这么多管理员感到困惑。

门户给你一个结果：已安装、失败或重试，但并不总是解释结果背后的逻辑。

如果不理解检测，人们会重新安装应用程序、重新打包安装程序或责怪设备。

而真正的问题往往只是一行检测逻辑。

第三，你看到了为什么终端验证很重要。

你确认了应用程序正常工作，你确认了用户未受影响。

这让你能够冷静且合乎逻辑地进行故障排除，而不是对红色状态做出反应。

最后，你练习了以正确的方式解决问题。

你没有卸载，也没有重新部署。

你修复了检测，并让Intune自行协调。

这就是只会浏览Intune的人与用Intune进行工程实践的人之间的区别。

如果你能像这样对Win32应用程序进行故障排除，现实世界中的Intune事件就不再可怕，它们变得可预测。

这才是真正的技能。

这也正是这个实验旨在教授的内容。

英文原文

Welcome to lab 7.2.

Break a Win32 app and troubleshoot.

Before we touch anything,let's reset expectations,because this lab is about mindset,not clicking buttons.

In Intune,deployment success is not determined by whether an app opens or runs.

Intune decides success based on one thing,detection logic.

So even if an app is actually installed and working on the device,Intune can still report it as failed if the detection rule cannot prove it.

And that's the big mindset shift for admins coming from SCCM or manual installs,where you usually trust what you see on the desktop.

Now here is why this matters in the real world.

A lot of production incidents start the same way.

Users say the app is there,but the portal says failed.

And if you don't understand detection,you will waste hours reinstalling or repackaging something that was not broken in the first place.

So in this lab,we are going to intentionally create the exact scenario.

We will break detection on purpose,safely,so we can learn to troubleshoot it properly.

Here is the flow we will follow and I want you to keep this structure in your head as we go.

First,we break detection.

Second,we observe what Intune reports in the portal.

Third,we compare that to endpoint reality,meaning is the app actually installed or not.

Then we prove root cause using evidence,including logs,instead of guessing.

And finally,we fix the issue correctly by restoring proper detection and confirming the status return to success.

One more important note,do not rush this lab.

This is exactly how real incidents unfold in production.

You will see conflicting signals,you will see delays in reporting and you will be tempted to just redeploy.

But the whole point here is to slow down and troubleshoot like an engineer.

What does Intune think?

Why does it think that?

And what does the endpoint prove?

If you learn this skill,Win32 troubleshooting becomes predictable,not stressful.

Alright,with that mindset set,let's start by breaking detection.

First,I'm going to open the Intune Admin Center,go to Apps and then Windows,and open our existing 7-Zip Win32 application from lab 7.1.

Now we are on the page,I'll go into the properties and then scroll to detection rules.

Click at it.

Here is the key detail.

Our detection method is MSI.

That means Intune checks the Windows installer database using the MSI product code.

And notice something important,when I click the three dots on the detection rule,I don't get an added option.

I only see delete.

That's not a bug.

That's normal for MSI detection rule in Intune.

If you need to change the MSI product code,you must delete the rule and create a new one.

So now we will break it.

I'll click the three dots and choose delete.

You'll also get a red banner saying a detection rule is required.

That means we do not have any detection rule in place.

At this point,Intune has no way to confirm whether 7-zip is installed.

Even if the app is physically present on the device,Intune cannot prove it.

Now let's add a fake detection rule.

Click Add.

This time,rule type should be file.

Please note,we are intentionally pointing detection to a fake path that does not exist.So Intune will fail detection even though the app is actually installed.

In the file or folder,I'll specify 7z.exe.

I'll leave associated with 32-bit app on 64-bit client to know and then click OK.

Once done,click on review plus save and then save.

In the next step,we will resync the endpoint,refresh the portal,and watch the failure appear.

And then we will troubleshoot it properly.

Let's switch back to our Windows endpoints.

Now,this step is unintentionally boring,and that's exactly why it's important.

This is where most admin gets uncomfortable because nothing appears to be happening.

But this waiting period is not wasted time.

It's how Intune really works.

Intune does not evaluate apps instantly.

There is no real-time install failed button behind the scenes.

Devices check on a schedule,they report state,and then Intune evaluates detection based on that data.

This delay is one of the main reasons when 32 troubleshooting feels confusing in real environments.

You change something in the portal and then you stare at the screen wondering if it worked.

And if you don't understand this timing model,you will think something is broken when it's actually just not evaluated yet.

Right now,remember what we did in the previous step,we didn't break the installer,we didn't uninstall the app,we only broke detection.

So what Intune is about to do is re-evaluate the device and ask a simple question,can I still detect this app?

And because detection is broken,Intune is going to conclude that the app is missing,even it's actually still installed on device.

This is the moment where portal reality and endpoint reality start to drift apart.

And the gap is where most troubleshooting mistakes happen.

So don't rush,don't redeploy,don't reinstall,just observe.

On Windows 10,we click on sync and do the same on our Windows 11.

Sync.

Once you trigger sync,do nothing else.

Do not reboot.

Do not open the app,just wait.

Wait at least 2-3 minutes.

And after 2-3 minutes,let's navigate back to Intune Admin Center.

Click on Apps,click on Windows and click on 7-Zip Win32.

Now pause here and really look at what Intune is telling you.

Notice the status has changed to failed.

And remember,we have not touched the app itself at all.

This is the key lesson.

Intune is not saying installation failed.

Intune is saying I cannot prove the app is installed.

That distinction matters more than almost anything else in Win32 troubleshooting.

Before jumping to conclusions,we always validate the endpoint.

Intune only tells us what detection logic thinks.

The device tells us what actually happened.

This step is critical because many real-world escalations start with the assumptions that fail means broken.

And that's often wrong.

We'll switch back to our Windows endpoints on our Windows 10 virtual machine.

Go to start and search for 7-zip.

Launch the 7-zip file manager and confirm the applications opens normally.

This is the key lesson.

The app is installed and working.

Only detection is broken.

Intune did not fail the install.

It failed the recognized success.

This exact mismatch is one of the most common causes of Intune support tickets.

Now we stop guessing and we prove what happened.

The Intune portal reports the outcome like installed,fail or retrying,but it does not always tell you why.

Logs are where the truth lives.

If you don't read logs,you end up reinstalling,repackaging,or blaming the device when the real issue is often something simple like detection logic.

In our case,we expect the install to be fine because the app opens.

So our goal is to find proof that Intune failed at detection step,not the install step.

At this point,we stop collecting data because we already have enough.

Let's summarize what we know.

Intune shows failed.

The app opens successfully.

There is no install error.

Logs are not explicitly pointing to an install failure.

So instead of chasing logs that don't help us here,we apply logic.

Intune has only three phases.

Download,install,detect.

Download happened.

Install happened.

So if Intune still says failed,only detection can be responsible.

Let's navigate back to Intune Admin Center.

And observe under the detection rule,it is pointing to a specific path.

The path was intentionally set to a non-existing location.

Ask this question out loud.

If this path does not exist,can detection ever return true?

Now we fix the only thing that was broken,that is detection logic.

Important reality check Intune does not instantly recover.

It does not immediately flip failed to succeed it.

And it does not reinstall the app just because detection is fixed.

What we are doing here is restoring the truth signal that Intune relies on.

We are telling Intune,this is how you correctly confirm the app exists.

Once detection is corrected,the app does not reinstall.

Intune re-evaluates detection during the next check-in and only then updates the status.

This delay is normal.

This is why admins think Intune is broken when it's just not asynchronous.

So under the detection rule,we will delete our fake path.

Then click on add again.

Run type MSI and we can see that MSI code has auto-detected original 7-zip product code.

We'll set MSI product version check to no and click OK.

And then we will review and save and save.

Now let's trigger sync from both our Windows endpoints.

Windows 10 sync and Windows 11.

Click sync.

Now let's wait 3 to 5 minutes.

Do not reinstall,do not force actions repeatedly.

Now after 3 to 5 minutes,go back to apps,Windows and click on 7-zip app.

And we can see that app status changes from failed to installed.

This is the most important lesson in Win32 app troubleshooting.

Fixing detection does not fix the past.It fixes the next evaluation.

If you expect instant recovery,you will think Intune is unreliable.

If you don't understand detection cycles,Intune becomes predictable.

This lab represents real in-tune engineering.

You learned why apps fail even when installed,why detection rules define truth,why endpoint validation matters,and why logs matter more than dashboards,how to fix issues without disrupting users,why logs matter more than dashboards,and how to fix issues without disrupting users.

This is the difference between someone who clicks button and someone who understands system.

With this scale,Win32 troubleshooting stop being scary.

Let's pause here and reflect on what you actually learned because this lab is more important than it looks.

This was not just about deploying 7-zip.

This lab showed you how to Intune really things.

First,you learn something critical.

An app can be installed and still be reported as failed.

That feels wrong at first,but now you understand why.

Intune does not check whether an app opens.

It does not care if users are happy.

It only asks one question.

Does the detection rule return true?

That's the first major shift in mindset.

Second,you learned why Win32 troubleshooting confuses so many admins.

The portal gives you a result,installed,failed,or retrying,but it does not always explain the logic behind the result.

Without understanding detection,people reinstall apps,repackage installers,or blame the device.

When the real issue is often a single line of detection logic.

Third,you saw why endpoint validation matters.

You confirmed the app worked,you confirmed users were not impacted.

That allowed you to troubleshoot calmly and logically instead of reacting to a red status.

Finally,you practiced fixing the issue the right way.

You did not uninstall,you did not redeploy.

You fixed detection and let Intune reconcile itself.

This is the difference between someone who clicks through Intune and someone who engineers with the Intune.

If you can troubleshoot Win32 apps like this,real-world Intune incidents stop being scary,they become predictable.

That's real skill.

And that's exactly what this lab was designed to teach.

74. 实验7-3：部署微软商店应用

欢迎进入实验七点三。

部署一个Microsoft Store应用程序。

在之前的实验中，我们处理了Win32应用程序，包括打包安装程序、构建检测规则，以及排查为何有时应用程序正常工作但Intune却报告失败。

在本实验中，我们将切换到新的Microsoft Store应用程序部署模型。

这是在Intune中部署应用程序最简单的方法。

无需打包，无需安装命令，也无需管理检测规则。

Intune直接从Microsoft Store拉取所有内容并为我们处理安装。

这里的目标不仅仅是安装一个应用程序，而是要理解在真实环境中，何时选择Store应用程序比Win32应用程序更合适。

随着我们进行实验，请注意设置和验证的速度有多快，以及故障排除的工作量减少了多少。

牢记这一点，让我们开始部署第一个Microsoft Store应用程序。

好了，在这一步中，我们将使用Microsoft Intune部署一个Microsoft Store应用程序。

这是Intune中可用的最简单的应用程序部署方法。

理解其背后的原因非常重要。

与Win32应用程序不同，Microsoft Store应用程序不需要我们打包任何东西。

没有Intune Win文件，没有内容准备工具，也没有需要我们担心的自定义检测规则。

Microsoft已经托管了该应用程序，管理安装逻辑，并为我们处理更新。

我们在Intune中的工作仅仅是选择应用程序、分配它，然后验证它在设备上是否正确安装。

这使得Microsoft应用程序非常适合标准用户应用程序、教室或培训环境，以及对可靠性至关重要的快速部署场景。

现在让我们从头开始。

我们将打开Intune管理中心，这里是进行所有设备、应用程序和策略管理的地方。

从左侧导航菜单中，选择“应用”。

此部分控制与应用程序部署和分配相关的所有内容。

接下来，点击“所有应用”。

这让我们可以完整查看当前由Intune管理的每个应用程序。

包括Win32应用程序、Store应用程序和Web应用程序。

在顶部菜单中，点击“创建”。

这是我们告诉Intune我们要向环境中引入新应用程序的方式。

此时，你应该看到“选择应用程序类型”屏幕。

在这里，我们选择要部署的应用程序类型。

在下一步中，我们将专门选择“Microsoft Store应用(新)”。

现在我们处于应用程序部署过程中一个非常重要的决策点。

这个屏幕看起来可能很简单，但我们在这里做出的选择完全改变了Intune期望我们完成的工作量。

Intune此刻问我们的不是想要哪个应用程序，而是我们正在部署哪种类型的应用程序。

这里的每个选项都代表不同的部署模型、不同的要求以及日后不同的故障排除工作量。

例如，如果我们部署的是Win32应用程序，我们需要下载安装程序，使用Win32内容准备工具对其进行打包，上传Intune Win文件，定义安装和卸载命令，并创建检测规则以证明安装成功。

这很强大，但也更复杂，更容易配置错误。

在本实验中，我们故意选择一种更简单、更可靠的方法。

这就是为什么我们选择“Microsoft Store应用(新)”。

当我们选择“Microsoft Store应用(新)”时，Intune会直接连接到Microsoft Store集成。

它会自动拉取应用程序元数据，如应用程序名称、发布者、版本控制和安装逻辑。

这意味着我们无需打包任何东西，无需上传文件，也无需编写检测规则。

Microsoft处理安装和更新，而Intune专注于分配和报告。

这种方法非常适合常见的生产力应用程序、培训和实验室环境，以及速度和可靠性比定制化更重要的场景。

因此，从“选择应用程序类型”下拉菜单中，选择“Microsoft Store应用(新)”，然后点击“选择”。

一旦你这样做，Intune会将你带到Store应用程序搜索和添加屏幕，我们可以在那里浏览或搜索想要部署的应用程序。

既然我们已经选择了“Microsoft Store应用(新)”，我们就通过Intune直接连接到了Microsoft Store目录。

这是一个重要的时刻，需要慢下来，因为我们在这里所做的代表了真实的生产工作流程。

这与你在企业环境中部署已批准应用程序所使用的流程相同。

在这个屏幕上，你会看到一个搜索Microsoft Store的选项。

这不是面向公众的消费者商店。

这是集成了Intune的目录，确保应用程序可以被正确部署、管理和报告。

对于本实验，我们将搜索“Microsoft To Do”。

为什么要选“Microsoft To Do”？

首先，它是一个免费的Microsoft应用程序，所以没有许可方面的复杂性。

其次，它很轻量，意味着安装快速且可靠。

第三，它让我们能够专注于学习Intune工作流程，而不是排查应用程序故障。

请点击“搜索Microsoft Store”。

在搜索框中，输入“Microsoft To Do”。

结果加载后，仔细从列表中选择“Microsoft To Do”。确保你选择的是Microsoft发布的正确应用程序，而不是名称相似的替代品。

这包括应用程序名称、发布者、应用程序标识符和类型。

请注意，我们没有上传文件，没有定义安装命令，也没有创建检测规则。

这是因为Microsoft在幕后处理了所有这些。

点击“选择”。

一旦你点击这里，应用程序就正式在Intune内部创建了。

从这一刻起，Intune将其视为任何其他受管应用程序，准备好进行配置、分配和报告。

你现在将被带到应用程序配置和分配屏幕，在这里我们决定谁获得该应用程序以及如何交付。

我们目前在“应用程序信息”页面。

这一步很重要，因为很多人会在这里匆忙行事，而在这里匆忙正是导致应用程序以错误的方式部署给错误的用户或在错误的时间部署的原因。

对于Microsoft Store应用程序，你会注意到大多数字段已经为我们填好了。

这包括应用程序名称、发布者、描述、包标识符和基本的安装行为。

Microsoft直接从Store拉取这些信息，这是使用Store应用程序的最大优势之一。

正因为如此，我们不需要上传安装程序，不需要定义安装或卸载命令，也不需要配置检测规则。

这些步骤是Win32应用程序所必需的，但在这里不需要。

在这个阶段，我们只是验证应用程序看起来正确，并且与我们打算部署的内容相符。

一旦你确认一切看起来都没问题，点击“下一步”继续。

现在我们位于“分配”页面。

这里是真正做决定的地方。

分配控制谁获得应用程序以及如何交付。

Intune给了我们三个主要选项。

“必需”：应用程序自动安装。

“可供已加入的设备使用”。

应用程序可通过公司门户可选安装。

以及“卸载”。

如果已安装，则删除该应用程序。

对于培训和学习环境，我强烈建议从“可用”开始。

为什么？

因为“可用”允许学习者打开公司门户，看到列出的应用程序，并选择自己安装。

这有助于他们同时理解管理员端和最终用户体验。

在生产环境中，通常使用“必需”，但对于实验室，“可用”能给我们更多的可见性和控制权。

在“可供已加入的设备使用”下，点击“添加组”并搜索我们的“Intune实验室用户”。

选择该组并点击“选择”。

一旦选中该组，点击“下一步”。

你将看到“查看加创建”页面。

花点时间在这里再次检查应用程序名称、分配类型和目标组。

如果一切看起来都正确，点击“创建”。

一旦应用程序创建成功，Intune将确认部署完成。

此时，该应用程序正式在Intune中可用。

但请记住，可用并不意味着立即安装。

设备仍然需要进行检查，公司门户也需要时间来刷新。

所以在下一步中，我们将切换到最终用户设备，并从公司门户一侧验证部署。

在接触终端之前，我们总是先验证Intune一侧。这一步节省时间，因为大多数“应用程序未安装”的问题实际上是分配或资格问题，而不是设备问题。

所以我们要确认两件事。

应用程序存在于Intune中并targeting正确的组。

我们正在测试的用户或设备实际上有资格接收它。

让我们现在就做。

转到“应用”，转到“所有应用”，然后在列表中找到并打开“Microsoft To Do”。

当应用程序打开时，不要直接跳到排查日志。

首先，我们确认target设置。

所以进入“属性”，在“分配”下，这是你要检查的内容。

在“可供已加入的设备使用”下，你是否看到了列出的正确组？

在我们的实验室中，我们应该在正确的部分看到“Intune Lab users”，通常是“可供已加入的设备使用”部分。

如果组缺失或者它在错误的部分，设备甚至永远不会尝试安装该应用程序。

而这正是人们浪费时间的地方。

当分配一直错误时，他们却在排查终端。

现在让我们转到“概述”页面，现在我们可以看到在“设备状态”和“用户状态”下，我们看到状态是“已安装”。

开始时看到“未安装”或“待定”是完全正常的。

这并不意味着失败。

这通常意味着设备尚未进行检查，公司门户尚未刷新，或者报告仍在追赶进度。

我们现在只想确认分配是可见且正确的，并且设备或用户出现在状态列表中。一旦你确认了这一点，我们再移动到终端，因为现在我们知道Intune配置正确。

现在我们离开Intune门户，从终端一侧验证所有内容。

这是整个实验中最重要的证明。

因为仪表板可能会撒谎，但设备永远不会。

请记住，我们将此应用程序分配为“可用”，而不是“必需”。

这意味着Intune不会在后台强制安装它。

相反，用户必须通过公司门户手动安装它，这正是真实企业环境中自助服务的工作方式。

这种模型通常用于可选的生产力应用程序、开发工具以及培训或沙盒环境。

在打开公司门户之前，我们将首先触发同步。

转到“设置”、“账户”、“访问工作或学校”。

转到“管理用户信息”并点击“同步”。

这会强制设备立即与Intune进行检查，而不是等待正常的同步周期。

现在在Windows设备上，打开公司门户。

输入“Company Portal”。

点击打开，如果你还没有在设备上安装公司门户，请前往Microsoft Store应用程序并从那里安装它。

一旦公司门户打开，转到“设置”并点击“同步”。

你会看到一条消息，指示你的设备正在同步以获取组织的最新更新。

这一步很重要。

如果不进行同步，即使分配正确，应用程序也可能尚未出现。

一旦同步完成，返回到“应用”部分。

如果一切配置正确，你现在应该看到“Microsoft To Do”列为可用应用程序。

如果没有立即看到，很多学习者会在此刻惊慌失措。

如果发生这种情况，请等待一分钟，刷新公司门户或再运行一次同步。

这种延迟是正常的，不是失败。

当“Microsoft To Do”出现时，点击它，然后点击“安装”。

此时，Intune将控制权移交给设备上的Microsoft Store服务。

一旦安装完成，状态将显示为“已安装”。

现在接下来是最后的验证步骤，这也是整个实验中最重要的步骤之一。

到目前为止，我们已经在Intune和公司门户中看到了信号，这些都很有用。

但请记住，这些门户报告的是意图，终端展示的才是现实。

所以在这一步中，我们不信任仪表板，我们要确认真实的、用户可见的证据，证明应用程序实际上已安装且可用。

让我们完全按照最终用户的方式来操作。

现在回到“开始”，搜索“Microsoft To Do”。

点击打开。

如果应用程序成功打开，这就是确凿的证据，证明Intune分配生效，公司门户安装完成，并且应用程序在终端上功能齐全。

这是在真实环境中预期的验证级别。

如果应用程序没有出现在搜索结果中或无法打开，这将立即告诉我们要在哪里进行故障排除。

但在这种情况下，成功启动意味着部署完成。

此时，Microsoft Store应用程序部署生命周期已完成，从Intune配置一直到真实的终端确认。

所以这就是使用Intune进行的Microsoft Store部署。

Store应用程序更快，因为没有打包，也没有像Win32那样的检测规则故障排除。

在真实环境中，尽可能使用Store应用程序，并将Win32留给第三方安装程序和复杂的企业应用程序。

英文原文

Welcome to lab 7.3.

Deploy a Microsoft Store app.

In the previous labs,we worked with Win32 apps,packaging installers,building detection rules,and troubleshooting why Intune sometimes reports failure even when apps work.

In this lab,we are switching to the Microsoft Store app new deployment model.

This is the simplest way to deploy apps in Intune.

There is no packaging,no install commands,and no detection rules to manage.

Intune pulls everything directly from the Microsoft Store and handles installation for us.

The goal here is not just to install an app,is to understand when store apps are the better choice compared to Win32 apps in real environments.

As we go through the lab,pay attention to how much faster setup and validation are and how much troubleshooting effort disappears.

With that in mind,let's start deploying our first Microsoft Store app.

Alright,in this step,we are going to deploy a Microsoft Store app using Microsoft Intune.

This is the simplest application deployment method available in Intune.

And it is important you understand why.

Unlike Win32 applications,Microsoft Store apps do not require us to package anything.

There is no Intune Win file,there is no content prep tool,and there are no custom detection rules for us to worry about.

Microsoft already hosts the app,manages the installation logic,and handles updates for us.

Our job in Intune is simply to select the app,assign it,and then verify it installs correctly on the device.

This makes Microsoft apps ideal for standard user applications,classroom or training environments,and quick deployments where reliability matters.

Now let's start from the beginning.

We are going to open the Intune Admin Center,which is where all device,app and policy management happens.

From the left-hand navigation menu,select apps.

This section controls everything related to application deployment and assignment.

Next,click on All Apps.

This gives us a complete view of every application currently managed by Intune.

Win32 apps,Store apps,and web apps.

In the top menu,click Create.

This is how we tell Intune we want to introduce a new application into the environment.

At this point,you should see the select app type screen.

This is where we choose what kind of application we are deploying.

And in the next step,we will specifically select Microsoft Store app.

Now we are at a very important decision point in the app deployment process.

This screen may look simple,but the choice we make here completely changes how much work Intune expects us to do.

What Intune is asking us right now is not which app we want,it is asking what type of app we are deploying.

Each option here represents a different deployment model,different requirements and different troubleshooting effort later.

For example,if we are deploying a Win32 app,we would need to download the installer,package it using Win32 content prep tool,upload the Intune Win file,define install and uninstall commands and create detection rules to prove installation success.

That's powerful,but it's also more complex and easier to misconfigure.

In this lab,we are intentionally choosing a simpler and more reliable method.

That's why we select Microsoft Store app new.

When we choose Microsoft Store app new,Intune connects directly to Microsoft Store integration.

It pulls the app metadata automatically,things like app name,publisher,versioning,and installation logic.

This means we don't package anything,we don't upload file and we don't write detection rules.

Microsoft handles installation and updates and Intune focuses on assignment and reporting.

This approach is ideal for common productivity apps,training and lab environments and scenarios where speed and reliability matter more than customization.

So from the select app type drop down,choose Microsoft Store app new and then click on select.

Once you do that,Intune will take you to the store app search and add screen where we can browse or search for the application we want to deploy.

Now that we have selected Microsoft Store app new,we are connected directly to Microsoft Store catalog through Intune.

This is an important moment to slow down because what we are doing here represents a real-world production workflow.

The same process you would use in an enterprise environment to deploy approved applications.

On this screen,you will see an option to search the Microsoft Store.

This is not the public consumer store.

This is the Intune integrated catalog,which ensures the app can be deployed,managed and reported on properly.

For this lab,we are going to search for Microsoft To Do.

Why Microsoft To Do?

First,it's a free Microsoft application,so there are no licensing complications.

Second,it's lightweight,meaning installation is quick and reliable.

And third,it allows us to focus on learning the Intune workflow,not troubleshooting app failures.

Go ahead and click search the Microsoft Store.

In the search box,type Microsoft To Do.

Once the results load,carefully select Microsoft To Do from the list.Make sure you're choosing the correct Microsoft published app and not a similarly named alternative.

This includes the application name,the publisher,the app identifier and the type.

Notice that we did not upload files,we did not define install commands and we did not create detection rules.

That's because Microsoft handles all of that behind the scenes.

Click on select.

Once you click this,the application is officially created inside Intune.

From this point forward,Intune treats it like any other managed application,ready for configuration,assignment and reporting.

You will now be taken into the app configuration and assignment screen,which is where we decide who gets the app and how it is delivered.

We are currently on the app information page.

This step is important because this is where many people rush and rushing here is how apps get deployed in the wrong way to the wrong users or at the wrong times.

For Microsoft Store apps,you will notice that most of these fields are already filled in for us.

That includes the app name,the publisher,description,package identifier and basic installation behavior.

Microsoft pulls this information directly from the store which is one of the biggest advantages of using store apps.

Because of that,we don't need to upload installers,define install or uninstall commands,or configure detection rules.

Those steps are required for Win32 apps,but not here.

At this stage,we are simply verifying that the app looks correct and matches what we intended to deploy.

Once you confirm that everything looks good,click Next to move forward.

Now we are on the assignment page.

This is where the real decision happens.

Assignment controls who gets the app and how it is delivered.

Intune gives us three main options.

Required,the app installs automatically.

Available for enrolled devices.

The app is optional via company portal.

And uninstall.

The app is removed if already installed.

For training and learning environments,I strongly recommend starting with available.

Why?

Because available allows learner to open the company portal,see the app listed and choose to install it themselves.

This helps them understand both the admin side and the end user experience.

In production environments,required is often used,but for labs,available gives us more visibility and control.

Under available for enrolled devices,click on add group and search for our Intune lab users.

Choose the group and click select.

Once the group is selected,click Next.

You will see the Review Plus Create page.

Take a moment here to double check the app name,the assignment type,and the target group.

If everything looks correct,click Create.

Once the app is created successfully,Intune will confirm that deployment is complete.

At this point,the app is officially available in Intune.

But remember,availability does not mean instant installation.

The device still needs to check in and the company portal needs time to refresh.

So in the next step,we will switch to the end user device and validate the deployment from the company portal side.

Before we touch the endpoint,we always validate the Intune side first.This step saves time because most app not installing issues are actually assignment or eligibility problems,not device problems.

So we are going to confirm two things.

The app exists in Intune and is targeted to the right group.

The user or device we are testing is actually eligible to receive it.

Let's do that now.

Go to apps,go to all apps and from the list,find an open Microsoft to-do.

When the app opens,don't jump straight to troubleshooting logs yet.

First,we confirm targeting.

So go into the properties and under the assignment,here is what you are checking.

Under available for enrolled devices,do you see the correct group listed?

In our lab,we should see Intune Lab users in the correct section,typically available for enrolled devices.

If the group is missing or it is in the wrong section,the device will never even attempt to install the app.

And this is exactly where people waste time.

They troubleshoot the endpoint when the assignment was wrong the whole time.

Now let's go to overview page and now we can see under the device status and user status,we see the status installed.

It is completely normal to see not installed or pending at the beginning.

That does not mean failure.

It usually means the device has not checked in yet,company portal has not refreshed or reporting is still catching up.

What we want to confirm right now is simply assignment is visible and correct and the device or user appears in the status list.Once you have confirmed that,then we move to the endpoint because now we know Intune is configured correctly.

Now we move away from the Intune portal and validate everything from the endpoint side.

This is the most important proof in the entire lab.

Because dashboards can lie,the device never does.

Remember,we assign this app as available,not required.

That means Intune will not force install it in the background.

Instead,the user must install it manually through the company portal,which is exactly how self-service works in real enterprise environments.

This model is commonly used for optional productivity apps,developer tools,and training or sandbox environments.

Before opening company portal,we will first trigger a sync.

Go to settings,accounts,access work or school.

Go to manage user info and click on sync.

This forces the device to immediately check in with the Intune instead of waiting for the normal sync cycle.

Now on the Windows device,open a company portal.

Type Company Portal.

Click and if you have not installed Company Portal on your device,please go to Microsoft Store Apps and install it from there.

Once Company Portal opens,go to Settings and click on Sync.

You will see a message indicating that your device is syncing to get the latest updates from your organizations.

This step is important.

Without syncing,the app may not appear yet,even though the assignment is correct.

Once the sync completes,go back to the app sections.

If everything is configured correctly,you should now see Microsoft To Do listed as an available application.

This is the moment where a lot of learners panic if they don't see it instantly.

If that happens,wait a minute,refresh Company Portal or run sync one more time.

This delay is normal,it's not a failure.

When the Microsoft To Do appears,click on that and then click on Install.

At this point,Intune hands control over to the Microsoft Store service on the device.

Once the install finishes,the status will show installed.

Now next is the final validation step and it's one of the most important ones in the entire lab.

Up to now,we have seen signals in Intune and Company Portal and those are useful.

But remember,this rule portals report intentions,endpoint shows reality.

So in this step,we are not trusting dashboards,we are confirming real user visible proof that the application is actually installed and usable.

Let's do this exactly the way an end user would.

Now go back to the start,search,Microsoft To Do.

Click to open.

If the application opens successfully,this is the conclusive proof that Intune assignment worked,company portal installations completed and the application is fully functional on the endpoint.

This is the level of validation expected in real environment.

If the app did not appear in search or failed to open,that would tell us immediately where to troubleshoot.

But in this case,successful launch means the deployment is complete.

At this point,Microsoft Store app deployment lifecycle is finished from Intune configuration all the way to real endpoint confirmation.

So that's a Microsoft Store deployment using Intune.

Store apps are faster because there is no packaging and no detection rule troubleshooting like Win32.

In real environments,use store apps whenever possible and reserve Win32 for third-party installers and complex enterprise apps.

75. 实验7-4：在iOS上配置应用保护策略（MAM）- Microsoft Edge案例

欢迎进入实验七点四：iOS版Microsoft Edge的应用程序保护策略（MAM）。到目前为止，我们的大多数Intune实验都集中在设备管理上，包括设备注册、合规性强制实施以及操作系统设置配置。

但个人设备的存在是不争的事实。

员工使用自己的手机，承包商使用自己的iPad，而组织仍然需要保护企业数据。

无需接管整个设备，这正是应用程序保护策略（也称为MAM）的设计初衷。

在本实验中，我们将把MAM应用于iOS版的Microsoft Edge。

我们将保护企业数据，要求设置工作PIN码，并防止这些数据被粘贴到个人应用中，而这一切都无需注册iPhone或iPad。

这是您在生产环境中最常看到的Intune实际用例之一。

好吧，让我们首先导航到Intune中应用程序保护策略的实际位置。

这一步看似简单，但理解这些策略为何位于此处至关重要。

在Intune中，任何与设备控制相关的内容，如配置配置文件、合规性策略和安全基线，都位于“设备”下。

但应用程序保护策略则不同。

MAM并非关于管理操作系统。

也不是关于控制手机本身。

而是关于控制企业数据在应用程序内部的行为。

这就是为什么保护策略位于“应用”下，而非“设备”下。

因此，从Intune管理中心出发，前往左侧导航栏并选择“应用”。

在“应用”下，选择“应用保护策略”。

此部分展示了跨平台（如Android和iOS）的所有现有应用保护策略。

在大型环境中，您通常会在此处看到多个策略，它们按平台、应用集或用户组进行划分。

现在我们将创建一个新策略。

点击“创建”。

Intune随即询问我们要保护哪个平台。

这一选择至关重要，因为应用保护策略是特定于平台的。

为Android创建的策略不适用于iOS，反之亦然。

由于本实验专注于保护iPhone和iPad上的数据，请选择“iOS或iPadOS”。

选定后，Intune将打开iOS应用保护策略向导，我们将在此开始定义如何在Microsoft Edge等应用内保护企业数据。

现在我们处于“基本信息”页面，这里是定义策略身份的地方。

这部分看似简单，但在真实组织中，清晰的命名是区分易于管理的Intune环境与混乱环境的最大因素之一。

因为一旦您拥有多个部门、不同的自带设备规则以及多个应用，您很快就会积累大量的MAM策略。

有些可能对高管更严格，有些对承包商较宽松，还有些可能仅适用于Outlook或Edge等特定应用。

因此，名称应让人一眼就能看出三件事。

第一，它是什么。

是MAM还是应用保护？

第二，哪个平台，本例中为iOS。

第三，哪个应用或用途，例如Edge和数据保护。

因此，在名称中输入“MAM iOS Edge数据保护”。

现在对于描述，保持简短但要有意义。

让您将其交给另一位管理员时，他们能立即明白此策略的用途。

所以我输入描述：“保护iOS版Microsoft Edge中的企业数据”。

该描述很重要，因为在许多租户中，策略名称可能会被缩短或遵循命名标准，而描述则是您记录业务意图的地方。

另外请注意，我们尚未在此定义强制执行措施。

我们只是在定义此策略是什么，以便Intune能正确跟踪它，并且我们可以大规模管理它。

设置好名称和描述后，点击“下一步”。

现在我们处于“应用”步骤。

现在我们处于应用保护策略向导的“应用”步骤，与旧的Intune文档相比，此屏幕略有变化。

因此，理解我们在此处的操作非常重要。

在顶部，您将看到“将策略目标设为”。

这决定了此应用保护策略将应用于哪些应用程序。

默认设置为“选定的应用”，对于本实验，这正是我们想要的。

我们并非将此策略应用于设备上的所有应用。

我们仅将其应用于特定的、支持Intune并能强制执行MAM控制的应用。

在“公共应用”下，选择“公共应用”。

这将打开支持接收应用保护策略的iOS应用程序列表。

请记住，应用保护策略仅适用于已启用Intune的应用。

如果某个应用未出现在列表中，则无法使用MAM对其进行保护。

因此，从列表中选择“Microsoft Edge”。

完成后，点击“选择”。

选定后，Edge将出现在“公共应用”下，确认它已成为此策略的目标。

请注意，我们在本实验中不添加自定义应用。

我们将范围保持紧凑并专注于单个应用，以便于理解和验证强制执行效果。

将“将策略目标设为”设置为“选定的应用”，并在“公共应用”下列出Microsoft Edge后，点击“下一步”。

现在我们处于“数据保护”页面，此屏幕完全是关于企业数据进入Microsoft Edge后如何允许移动。

这还不是身份验证。

我们尚未在此设置PIN码。

此步骤严格定义数据丢失防护行为，也称为DLP。

在顶部，注意标记为“数据传输”的部分。

这些设置控制当企业数据试图被复制、粘贴、共享、保存或在另一个应用中打开时会发生什么。

首先查看“将所有数据发送到其他应用”。

对于安全的自带设备场景，将其设置为“策略管理的应用”。

这意味着企业数据只能在其他受Intune保护的应用之间移动，而不能进入Notes、WhatsApp或Gmail等个人应用。

接下来，审查“保存组织数据的副本”。

将其设置为“阻止”。

这可防止企业内容被保存到非托管位置，如个人存储或第三方应用。

现在向下滚动到“限制在其他应用之间剪切、复制和粘贴”。

将其设置为“允许粘贴到策略管理的应用”。

这允许仅在托管应用之间进行复制和粘贴，同时阻止粘贴到个人应用中。

接下来，查看“加密组织数据”。

将其设置为“要求”。

这确保受保护应用处理的企业数据在设备上静态加密。

此页面上的其他所有内容，如打印、截图、通知，在本实验中可保留默认值。

我们保持策略聚焦，以便于理解和验证强制执行效果。

配置好这些数据保护控制后，点击“下一步”。

现在我们处于“访问要求”步骤，此页面回答一个非常具体的问题。

用户在访问应用内的企业数据之前如何进行身份验证？

请记住，这不是设备通行码。

这是仅针对受保护应用（如Microsoft Edge）强制执行的工作级访问控制。

在顶部，启用“访问PIN码”并设置为“要求”。

这意味着即使iPhone本身已解锁，用户在访问应用内的企业数据之前仍必须进行身份验证。

对于PIN码类型，保留为“数字”。

这使实验保持简单，同时仍能反映真实的企业行为。

接下来，在“简单PIN码”下，保留为“允许”，并将最小PIN码长度设置为四。

四位PIN码足以演示强制执行效果，而不会在测试期间让用户感到沮丧。

现在查看生物识别选项。

对于触控ID和面容ID，均保留为“允许”。

这允许用户使用生物识别快速进行身份验证，同时PIN码仍作为底层安全控制存在。

注意“超时后用PIN码覆盖生物识别”的设置。

将其保留为“要求”，超时时间设为三十分钟。

这确保即使使用了生物识别，用户也将定期被要求重新输入工作PIN码，从而强化这是受保护的企业访问的概念。对于“当设置设备PIN码时的应用PIN码”，保留为“要求”。

这是一个重要点。

即使设备已有通行码，Intune仍会为企业数据强制执行单独的应用级PIN码。

最后，将“用于访问的工作或学校账户凭据”设置为“不需要”。

我们希望身份验证顺畅且一致，避免在实验期间强制重复登录。

有了这些访问要求，我们现在已完全定义了谁可以访问企业数据以及他们如何在应用内进行身份验证。

点击“下一步”继续进入“条件启动”，我们将在此定义如果未满足访问条件会发生什么。

现在我们处于“条件启动”步骤，此页面定义了当未满足访问条件时会发生什么。

到目前为止，我们已定义了哪些应用受保护、企业数据如何行为以及用户如何身份验证。

“条件启动”回答了最后一个问题。

如果出现问题，Intune应该做什么？

将此视为应用保护策略的强制执行和后果层。

在顶部，“应用条件”下，查看“最大PIN码尝试次数”。

此设置控制在Intune采取行动之前，用户可以输入错误应用PIN码的次数。

将“最大PIN码尝试次数”设置为五，并将操作保留为“重置PIN码”。

这意味着如果用户反复身份验证失败，Intune将强制其在再次访问企业数据之前重置工作PIN码。

接下来，查看“离线宽限期”。

第一个值一千四百四十分钟代表应用在访问被阻止之前可以离线使用的时长。

将其保留为“阻止访问分钟数”。

这确保应用必须定期与Intune回连以确认策略仍然有效。

在其下方，注意第二个离线宽限期设置为九十天，操作为“擦除数据”。

这是一个更强的控制措施。

如果应用长时间未回连，Intune将自动从应用中移除企业数据。

现在向下滚动到“设备条件”。

在这里，我们基于设备风险定义条件。

即使设备未注册，对于越狱或Root的设备，将操作保留为“阻止访问”。

这意味着如果Intune检测到设备受损，应用内对企业数据的访问将立即被阻止。

这些设置共同强制执行明确的安全姿态。

处理反复的身份验证失败。

控制离线访问，并自动阻止受损设备。

设置好这些条件启动规则后，点击“下一步”。

现在我们处于“分配”步骤，这是应用保护策略实际生效的地方。

到目前为止，我们所做的一切都只是在定义规则：哪些应用受保护、数据如何行为、用户如何身份验证以及如果未满足条件会发生什么。

但在我们分配策略之前，不会执行任何强制措施。

在顶部，您将看到“包含的组”。

应用保护策略几乎总是基于用户的，而非基于设备的。

这是因为MAM跟随用户的身份，无论他们使用哪部个人手机或平板电脑。

在“包含的组”下点击“添加组”，查找“Intune实验用户”组并选择。

选定后，确认该组出现在“包含的组”下。

请注意，我们在本实验中不添加任何“排除的组”。

在真实环境中，排除项通常用于应急账户或特殊用户，但为了学习和验证，保持简单有助于我们清晰地看到强制执行效果。

另请注意屏幕上的消息：您不能在“包含”和“排除”之间混合用户组和设备组。

这强化了一个重要概念。

此策略关乎用户和应用，而非设备所有权。

一旦包含了正确的组且未设置排除项，点击“下一步”。

现在检查您的设置。

如果一切看起来正确，点击“创建”。

既然我们的iOS策略已出现，现在我们将离开Intune门户并切换到iPhone或iPad，因为这才是实际验证应用保护策略的地方。

这一步至关重要，也是许多人无意中破坏实验的地方。

这里的关键规则非常简单，我希望您清楚地记住它。

我们登录公司门户，但不注册设备。

注册会将此转变为MDM场景，但本实验是关于无注册的MAM，这正是组织在个人设备上保护数据的方式。

因此，在iPhone或iPad上打开Intune公司门户应用。

使用测试账户登录。

登录后，iOS可能会提示您设置设备管理、安装管理配置文件或将此设备安全工作化。

如果您看到任何此类提示，请勿继续。

根据屏幕上显示的措辞，选择“跳过”、“取消”或“暂不”。

这是预期行为。

公司门户总是试图引导用户进行注册，但对于MAM测试，我们有意拒绝它。

因此在屏幕上，我将点击“推迟”并选择推迟。

我们想要的是这种状态。

用户已登录，账户已注册，但设备保持个人且未受管状态。

您通常可以通过检查未安装管理配置文件、设备未显示为已在Intune中注册、公司门户显示有限访问而非完全管理来确认这一点。

一旦用户登录且设备保持未注册状态，我们就准备好了。

此时，应用保护策略能够仅基于用户身份在支持的应用内生效。

现在我们到达本实验最重要的部分：验证。

到目前为止，我们所做的一切都是配置。

这一步是我们确认应用保护策略实际上正在未注册的个人iOS设备上工作的地方。

拿起您的iPhone或iPad并打开Microsoft Edge。

确保使用与我们用于策略分配的相同工作账户登录。

例如，测试用户。

我将输入测试用户电子邮件。

点击“下一步”。

系统提示Edge想要打开Authenticator。

点击“打开”。

我们可以看到我们已使用测试用户零一登录。

登录后，浏览至企业资源。

在本例中，我将打开我的OneDrive，登录，输入我的test.user01然后点击“下一步”。

我将输入密码并登录。

对于不保存密码，我将点击“暂不”。

好吧，让我们在此暂停片刻，因为我们现在看到的并不是错误。

也不是实验坏了。

此屏幕来自Microsoft Entra ID，而非Intune。

它实际上正在完全按照配置执行操作。

消息显示“设置您的设备以获取访问权限”，这告诉我们一件非常重要的事情。

在Intune应用保护策略甚至有机会生效之前，Entra ID会评估条件访问。

在此租户中，存在一个条件访问策略，要求满足以下其中之一。

设备必须已注册，或者设备必须标记为合规。

该要求在许多生产环境中是完全有效的，但它阻碍了仅MAM的场景。

请记住，本实验是关于无设备注册的应用保护。

这意味着没有MDM，没有合规性策略，没有设备信任，只有基于应用内身份的保护。

由于条件访问当前正在强制执行设备要求，Entra ID永远不会为Edge或OneDrive颁发令牌。

因此Intune甚至从未获得控制权。

这就是为什么点击“推迟”不起作用的原因。

Edge不断循环，应用不断要求设备设置。

这实际上是一个非常真实的企业场景。

许多组织想要MAM，但忘记了条件访问必须明确允许它。

因此，这里的修复不在Edge中，不在公司门户中，也不在应用保护策略中。

修复方法是更新条件访问，以允许MAM。

所以在下一步中，我们将创建一个专门的条件访问策略，该策略不要求设备管理，而是要求应用保护策略。

一旦到位，登录将成功，不再强制注册，应用保护将完全按设计工作。

这很好地提醒我们，MAM和条件访问必须一起设计，否则，即使是完美的Intune策略也不会生效。

因此，我们将导航至Entra管理中心。

在创建条件访问策略之前，我们总是先暂停并审查租户中已存在的内容。

条件访问策略是一起评估的，而非孤立评估。

这意味着如果一个策略要求的內容其他策略没有，该要求仍然适用。

特别是，有三个条件访问要求会完全破坏仅MAM的场景。

第一，要求设备标记为合规。

第二，要求设备注册。

第三，要求混合Azure AD加入。

如果为用户或应用强制实施了其中任何一项，那么仅MAM访问将永远无法工作，无论我们的新应用保护或条件访问策略多么正确。

因此，第一步始终是识别是否已存在此类策略。

在生产环境中，我们不会简单地删除这些策略，而是调整范围、添加排除项，或专门为MAM用户创建单独的条件访问策略。

在此实验环境中，我识别出一个现有的条件访问策略，要求设备合规。

由于这是一个控制实验，我删除了该策略，以便我们能清晰地演示无设备注册的应用保护。

重要的是要理解，删除冲突的策略只是移除了障碍，但本身并不能启用MAM。

要实际强制执行应用保护，我们仍然需要一个明确要求应用保护策略的条件访问策略。

此审查步骤至关重要。

大多数条件访问问题并非由您正在创建的策略引起，而是由您忘记检查的另一策略引起。

现在我们将创建一个条件访问策略，这是允许无设备注册的MAM工作的缺失环节。

条件访问在Intune应用保护策略之前运行。

因此，如果条件访问要求设备合规或受管，仅MAM访问将被阻止，这正是我们刚刚看到的。

因此在Entra ID下，进入“条件访问”并点击“创建新策略”。

在此屏幕上，我们正在创建一个新的条件访问策略，它是基于用户的、特定于应用的，且不要求设备注册。

我们将此策略分配给我们的测试用户，目标是Microsoft应用（如OneDrive和SharePoint），并且我们不要求合规设备，而是要求应用保护策略。

这告诉Entra ID：允许访问企业数据，但仅通过受保护的应用。

一旦此策略到位，登录将成功，不再强制注册，Intune MAM最终将接管。

我将其命名为“CA允许无设备注册的MAM”。

我将选择用户，选择“用户和组”，然后再次勾选“用户和组”并查找用户组。

“Intune实验用户”，选择。

然后接下来我将进入“目标资源”，选择“所有资源”（以前称为所有云应用）。

进入“条件”并进入“设备平台”。将策略应用于“选定的设备平台”。

我将配置为“是”。

在“包含”下，选择“设备平台”。我将选择iOS，并将“排除”留空。

点击“完成”。

然后点击“授予”。

选择“授予访问权限”，并勾选“要求应用保护策略”。

对于多个控件，我将选择“需要以下选定控件之一”，然后点击“选择”。

完成后，将“启用策略”设置为“开”，然后点击“创建”。

一旦创建了条件访问策略，请等待二到五分钟。

条件访问策略应用很快，但移动令牌有时会略有延迟。

好吧，现在让我们切换到iOS设备。

在我们继续之前，有一个重要的准备步骤我们需要做，这一步是有意为之的。

在iPhone或iPad上，完全删除Microsoft Edge应用，然后重启设备。

一旦设备重新启动，从App Store重新安装Microsoft Edge。

我们这样做的原因很简单。

在iOS上，Edge非常积极地缓存登录令牌和保护状态。

如果在我们的条件访问和应用保护策略完全对齐之前Edge已登录，它可能会陷入未受保护的上下文中。

删除Edge并重启将清除所有身份验证令牌、缓存的应用保护状态以及任何未完成的MAM握手。

这保证Edge从头开始，并被迫正确评估我们的策略。

一旦重新安装Edge，打开它并直接前往portal.office.com。

在我们接触任何企业数据之前，iOS可能会显示一条消息，称此应用正由您的组织管理。

这很重要，因为设备本身仍然是个人的，但应用程序现在正在受保护的工作上下文中运行。

然后系统要求我们创建工作PIN码。

此PIN码不是设备通行码。

也不是Apple ID密码。

这是由Intune强制执行的单独应用级控制。

这正是应用保护策略生效的时刻。

从这里开始，在此应用内访问的任何企业数据都受到保护，无论设备所有者是谁。

让我们设置PIN码，然后确认您的PIN码。

系统询问是否将Microsoft设置为默认浏览器。

我说“不，谢谢”。

现在让我们前往我们的OneDrive，登录，继续登录，我将在此输入我们的测试用户，然后点击“下一步”。

现在我有一个名为“MAMWIP测试数据”的测试文件。

让我们打开它。

这是该文件内的内容。

我将复制数据并前往我的“备忘录”，我们将尝试在此粘贴。

这正是应用保护策略展示其真正价值的地方。

如果一切正常工作，将发生以下两种情况之一。

粘贴操作被完全阻止，或者您看到类似“无法在此处粘贴您组织的数据”的消息。

该消息并非来自Edge，而是来自Intune应用保护，在应用级别强制执行。

这确认了三件关键事情。

第一，设备未注册。

第二，用户身份触发了策略。

第三，即使在个人手机上，企业数据也受到保护。

这就是MAM完全按设计工作。

在本实验中，您成功地在未注册的个人iOS设备上保护了企业数据。

您创建了iOS应用保护策略，以Microsoft Edge为目标，强制执行了工作PIN码，并防止企业数据被粘贴到个人应用中。

这是现代自带设备安全性的实际运作。

用户完全控制其个人设备，而组织控制其企业数据。

这就是应用层的零信任模样。

我们默认不信任设备。

我们基于身份和上下文保护数据。

如果您能设计、部署并验证此策略，您现在就理解了真实企业环境中使用最广泛且最有效的Intune安全功能之一。

Welcome to lab 7.4 App Protection Policy MAM on iOS Microsoft Edge Until now most of our Intune labs focused on device management,enrolling devices,enforcing compliance and configuring operating system settings.

But personal devices are a reality.

Employees use their own phones,contractors use their own iPads and organizations still need to protect corporate data.

Without taking over the entire device,that's exactly what app protection policies,also called MAM,are designed for.

In this lab,we will apply MAM to Microsoft Edge on iOS.

We will protect corporate data,require a work pin,and prevent that data from being pasted into personal apps,all without enrolling the iPhone or iPad.

This is one of the most common real-world Intune use cases you will see in protection environments.

Alright,let's start by navigating to where app protection policies actually live in Intune.

This step may feel simple,but it's important to understand why these policies are located where they are.

In Intune,anything related to device control,things like configuration profiles,compliance policies,and security baselines live under devices.

But app protection policies are different.

MAM is not about managing the operating system.

It's not about controlling the phone itself.

It's about controlling what happens to corporate data inside applications.

That's why protection policies live under apps,not devices.

So from the Intune Admin Center,go to the left-hand navigation and select Apps.

And under the apps,select App Protection Policies.

This section shows you all existing app protection policies across platforms such as Android and iOS.

In larger environments,you will often see multiple policies here separated by platform,app set or user group.

Now we will create a new policy.

Click on Create.

Intune now asks you which platform we want to protect.

This choice is critical because app protection policies are platform specific.

A policy created for Android will not apply to iOS and vice versa.

Since this lab is focused on protecting data on iPhones and iPads,select iOS or iPad OS.

Once selected,Intune opens the iOS app protection policy visit where we will begin defining how corporate data is protected inside apps like Microsoft Edge.

Now we are on the basic page and this is where we define the identity of the policy.

This part looks simple,but in real organizations clear naming is one of the biggest differences between an in-tuned environment that easy to manage and one that becomes a mess.

Because once you have multiple departments,different bring your own devices rules,and multiple apps,you quickly end up with lots of MAM policies.

Some might be stricter for executives,some might be lighter for contractors,and some might only apply to specific apps like Outlook or Edge.

So the name should tell you at a glance three things.

First,what it is.

Is it MAM or app protection?

Second,which platform,which is iOS in this case.

And third,which app or purpose,such as Edge and Data Protection.

So for name,enter MAM iOS Edge Data Protection.

Now for the description,keep it short but meaningful.

Something you could hand to another admin and they understand instantly what this policy is for.

So I'll type a description protect corporate data in Microsoft Edge on iOS.

That description is important because in many tenants the policy name might be shortened or follow a naming standard and the description is where you capture the business intent.

Also notice we are not defining enforcement here yet.

We are just defining what this policy is so Intune can track it properly and we can manage it at scale.

Once the name and descriptions are set,click next.

Now we are on the app step.

Now we are on the app step of the app protection policy visit and this screen has changed slightly compared to older Intune documentation.

So it's important we understand that we are doing here.

At the top you will see target policy 2.

This determines which applications this app protection policy will apply to.

By default it's set to selected apps and for this lab that's exactly what we want.

We are not applying this policy to all apps on the device.

We are applying it only specific Intune aware apps that can enforce MAM controls.

Under Public Apps select public apps.

This opens the list of supported iOS applications that can receive app protection policies.

Remember app protection policies only work with apps that are Intune enabled.

If an app does not appear in the list,it cannot be protected using MAM.

So from the list,select Microsoft Edge.

So once done,click select.

Once selected,Edge appears under the public app confirming that it's now targeted by this policy.

Notice that we are not adding custom apps in this lab.

We are keeping the scope tight and focused on a single app so enforcement is easy to understand and validate.

With Target Policy 2 set to selected apps and Microsoft Edge listed under public apps,click Next.

Now we are on the data protection page,and this screen is all about how corporate data is allowed to move once it's inside Microsoft Edge.

This is not authentication yet.

We are not setting a pin here.

This step strictly defines data loss prevention behavior,also called TLP.

At the top,notice the section labeled data transfer.

These settings control what happens when corporate data tries to be copied,be pasted,be shared,be saved,or be opened in another app.

First look at send all data to the other apps.

For a secure bring your own device scenario,set this to policy managed apps.

This means corporate data can only move between other Intune protected apps,not into personal apps like Nodes,WhatsApp or Gmail.

Next,review save copies of organization data.

Set this to block.

This prevents corporate content from being saved into unmanaged locations like personal storage or third-party apps.

Now scroll down to restrict cut,copy and paste between other apps.

Set this to policy manage app with paste in.

This allows copy and paste only within managed apps while blocking paste into personal apps.

Next,look at encrypt organization data.

Set this to require.

This ensures corporate data handled by protected apps is encrypted at rest on the device.

Everything else on this page,printing,screenshots,notifications can remain at their default values for this lab.

We are keeping the policy focused so enforcement is easy to understand and validate.

Once these data protection controls are configured,click Next.

Now we are on the access requirement step and this page answers one very specific question.

How does a user authenticate before accessing corporate data inside the app?

Remember,this is not the device passcode.

This is a work-level access control enforced only for protected apps like Microsoft Edge.

At the top,enable pin for access and set to require.

This means that even if the iPhone itself is unlocked,the user must still authenticate before accessing corporate data inside the app.

For the pin type,leave it set to numeric.

This keeps the lab simple while still reflecting real enterprise behavior.

Next,under the simple pin,keep this set to allow and set the minimum pin length to 4.

A 4 digit pin is enough to demonstrate enforcement without frustrating users during testing.

Now look at the biometric options.

For both touch ID and face ID,leave these set to allow.

This allows user to authenticate quickly using biometrics while the pin still exists as the underlying security control.

Notice the setting override biometric with pin after timeout.

leave this set to require with a time out of thirty minutes.

This ensures that even if biometrics are used,the user will periodically be required to re enter the work pin,reinforcing that this is protected corporate access For app pin,when device pin is set,leave this to require.

This is an important point.

Even if the device already has a passcode,Intune still enforces a separate app level pin for corporate data.

Finally,leave work or school account credentials for access set to not required.

We want authentication to be smooth and consistent without forcing repeated sign-ins during the lab.

With these access requirements in place,we have now fully defined who can access corporate data and how they authenticate inside the app.

Click Next to continue to conditional launch,where we will define what happens if access conditions are not met.

Now we are on the conditional launch step and this page defines what happens when access conditions are not met.

Up to this point,we have defined which app is protected,how corporate data behaves and how the user authenticates.

Conditional launch answers the final question.

What should Intune do if something goes wrong?

Think of this as the enforcement and consequence layer of app protection policies.

At the top,under app conditions,look at maximum pin attempts.

This settings controls how many times a user can enter the wrong app pin before Intune takes action.

Set maximum pin attempts to 5 and leave the action as reset pin.

This means if a user repeatedly fails authentication,Intune forces them to reset the work pin before accessing corporate data again.

Next,look at offline grace period.

The first value 1440 minutes represents how long the app can be used offline before access is blocked.

Leave this set as blocked access minutes.

This ensures the app must periodically check back in with Intune to confirm the policy is still valid.

Below that,notice a second offline grace period set to 90 days with the action wipe data.

This is a stronger control.

If the app has not checked in for a long time,Intune will automatically remove corporate data from the app.

Now scroll down to device conditions.

Here we are defining conditions based on a device risk.

Even though the device is not enrolled,for jailbroken or root devices,leave the action set to block access.

This means if Intune detects the device is compromised,access to corporate data inside the app is immediately blocked.

These settings together enforce a clear security posture.

Repeated authentication failures are handled.

Offline access is controlled and compromised devices are blocked automatically.

Once these conditional launch rules are set,click Next.

Now we are on the assignment step and this is where the app protection policy actually becomes active.

Up to now everything we have done was just defining rules,what apps are protected,how data behaves,how users authenticate and what happens if conditions are not met.

But nothing is enforced until we assign the policy.

At the top you will see included groups.

App protection policies are almost always user-based,not device-based.

That's because MAM follows the user's identity regardless of which personal phone or tablet they use.

Click Add Groups under included groups and look for Intune Lab Users group and select.

Once selected,confirm the group appears under included groups.

Notice that we are not adding any excluded groups for this lab.

In real environments,exclusions are often used for break class accounts or special users,but for learning and validation,keeping this simple helps us clearly see enforcement.

Also note the message on the screen,you cannot mix user and device groups across include and exclude.

That reinforces an important concept.

This policy is about user and applications,not device ownership.

Once the correct group is included and no exclusions are set,click next.

Now review the settings.

If everything looks correct,click create.

And now that our iOS policy has appeared,now we move away from the Intune portal and switch to the iPhone or iPad because this is where app protection policies are actually validated.

This step is critical and it is also where many people accidentally break the lab.

The key rule here is very simple and I want you to remember this clearly.

We sign into Company Portal but we do not enroll the device.

Enrollment would turn this into a MDM scenario but this lab is about MAM without enrollment which is how organizations protect data on personal devices.

So on the iPhone or iPad open the Intune Company Portal app.

What we want is this state.

The user is signed in,the account is registered,but the device remains personal and unmanaged.

Once the user is signed in and the device remains unenrolled,we are ready.

At this point,the app protection policy is capable of applying inside supported apps purely based on user identity.

Alright,let's pause here for a moment because what we are seeing right now is not a mistake.

It's not a broken lab.

This screen is coming from Microsoft EntroId,not from Intune.

And it's actually doing exactly what it was configured to do.

The message says set up your device to get access and that tells us something very important.

Before Intune app protection policies even get a chance to apply,EntroId evaluates conditional access.

And in this tenant,there is a conditional access policy that requires one of the following.

The device must be enrolled or the device must be marked as compliant.

That requirement is perfectly valid in many production environments,but it blocks MAM-only scenarios.

Remember,this lab is about app protection without device enrollment.

That means no MDM,no compliance policy,no device trust,just identity-based protection inside the app.

Because conditional access is currently enforcing a device requirement,EntroID never issues a token for Edge or OneDrive.

So Intune never even gets control.

That's why tapping postpone does not work.

Edge keeps looping and the app keeps asking for device setup.

This is actually a very real enterprise scenario.

Many organizations want MAM but forget that conditional access must explicitly allow it.

So the fix here is not in edge,it's not in company portal and it's not in the app protection policy.

The fix is to update conditional access so that MAM is allowed.

So in the next step,we are going to create a just or conditional access policy that does not require device management and instead requires an app protection policy.

Once that is in place,the sign-in will succeed,enrollment will no longer be forced,and app protection will work exactly as designed.

This is a great reminder that MAM and conditional access must be designed together,otherwise,even a perfect in-tune policy will not apply.

So we will navigate to Entra Admin Center.

Before creating a conditional access policy,we always pause and review what already exists in the tenant.

Conditional access policies are evaluated together,not in isolation.

That means if one policy requires something the others don't,that requirement still applies.

In particular,there are three conditional access requirements that completely break a MAM-only scenario.

First,require device to be marked as compliant.

Second,require device enrollment.

And third,require hybrid Azure AD join.

If any of those are enforced for the user or the app,then MAM-only access will never work,no matter how correct our new app protection or conditional access policy is.

So the first step is always to identify whether such a policy already exists.

In production environments,we don't simply delete these policies,instead we adjust scope,add exclusions,or create a separate conditional access policy specifically for MAM users.

In this lab environment,I identified an existing conditional access policy that required devices to be compliant.

Because this is a control lab,I removed the policy so we could clearly demonstrate app protection without device enrollment.

It's important to understand that deleting a conflicting policy removes a blocker,but it does not enable MAM by itself.

To actually enforce app protection,we still need a conditional access policy that explicitly requires an app protection policy.

This review step is critical.

Most conditional access issues are not caused by the policy you are creating,they are caused by another policy you forgot to check.

Now we are going to create a conditional access policy and this is the missing piece that allows MAM without device enrollment to work.

Conditional access runs before Intune app protection policies.

So if conditional access requires a compliant or managed device,MAM only access is blocked,which is exactly what we just saw.

So under the Enter ID,go to Conditional Access and click on Create New Policy.

On this screen,we are creating a new conditional access policy that is user-based,app specific and does not require device enrollment.

This tells Enter ID,allow access to corporate data but only through protected apps.

Alright,let's now switch over to the iOS device.

On the iPhone or iPad,delete the Microsoft Edge app completely,then reboot the device.

Once the device is back up,reinstall Microsoft Edge from the App Store.

The reason we do this is simple.

On iOS,Edge caches sign-in tokens and protection state very aggressively.

If Edge was signed in before our conditional access and app protection policies were fully aligned,it can get stuck in an unprotected context.

Deleting Edge and rebooting clears all authentication tokens,cached app protection state,and any incomplete MAM handshakes.

This guarantees that Edge starts fresh and is forced to evaluate our policies correctly.

It's not an Apple ID password.

It's a separate application level control enforced by Intune.

This is the exact moment app protection policies take effect.

From here on,any corporate data accessed inside this app is protected,regardless of who owns the device.

First,the device is not enrolled.

Second,the user identity triggered the policy.

And third,corporate data is being protected even on a personal phone.

That's MAM working exactly as designed.

In this lab,you successfully protected corporate data on a personal iOS device without enrolling it.

You created an iOS app protection policy,targeted Microsoft Edge,enforced a work pin,and prevented corporate data from being pasted into personal applications.

This is modern bring your own device security in action.

Users keep full control of their personal device,and organization keeps control of their corporate data.

And this is what zero trust looks like at the application layer.

We don't trust the device by default.

We protect the data based on identity and context.

76. 总结

让我们来总结第七部分，并将我们所学的内容串联起来。

在本部分中，您学习了Intune如何端到端地处理应用程序，从企业级的Win32应用部署开始。

您不仅仅是部署了一个应用，而是正确地打包了它，定义了检测逻辑，有意识地进行分配，并以正确的方式在终端上进行了验证。

您还看到了分配方式如何直接塑造用户体验。

“必需”与“可用”，“基于用户”与“基于设备”。

这些并非技术开关，而是影响生产力、信任度以及支持工单量的决策。

随后，我们进入了真实的故障排除环节，您故意破坏了一个Win32应用并诊断了其故障原因。

这至关重要，因为在生产环境中，大多数问题并非应用本身损坏，而是逻辑出错。

最后，您学习了如何使用应用程序保护策略来保护企业数据，而无需管理设备本身。

这是安全“自带设备”架构与现代零信任的基石：保护的是数据，而不仅仅是设备。

到第七部分结束时，您现在已全方位理解了Intune中的应用程序管理。

涵盖部署、体验、故障排除以及数据保护。

在下一部分中，我们将深入探讨终端安全，通过攻击面缩减、防火墙控制和权限管理来加固设备，并将所有内容回归到零信任强制执行上来。

You did not just deploy an app,you packaged it correctly,defined detection logic,assigned it intentionally,and validated it on the endpoint the right way.

Required versus available,user-based versus device-based.

This is critical because in production environments,most issues are not broken apps,they are broken logic.

ASR并非在恶意软件运行后做出反应，而是减少攻击者甚至无法启动攻击的途径数量。

接下来，我们将重点关注Microsoft Defender防火墙。

许多人低估了基于主机的防火墙，但在零信任架构中，每个终端都必须具备自我防御能力。

这是现代终端安全中最强大的控制措施之一。

您将了解为何永久性的本地管理员权限存在危险，以及终端权限管理（简称EPM）如何在移除常驻特权的同时，允许用户在需要时安全地提升权限。

这将大幅降低网络钓鱼、恶意软件以及用户操作失误所带来的影响。

此后，我们将通过零信任视角将所有内容串联起来。

ASR、防火墙和EPM并非孤立的功能。

它们协同工作，以缩减攻击面、限制横向移动，并在设备遭到泄露时将损害降至最低。

最后，我们将专注于验证与故障排除。

您将学习如何确认这些安全控制措施是否已在设备上实际激活，如何正确解读策略状态，以及当安全策略未按预期运行时如何诊断问题。

到第八部分结束时，您将能够使用Intune设计并强制执行强大的预防性终端安全控制措施，这些措施能在早期阻止攻击并大幅降低风险。

让我们从主题八点一开始，清晰理解攻击面缩减的真正含义。

In this section,we move deeper into endpoint security hardening.

This is where Intune stops being just a management platform and becomes a preventive security control.

In section 8,we focus on preventing attacks before they happen.

So far in this course,you have seen how Intune evaluates device health,enforces access,and responds to threats.

Now we shift left into hardening the endpoint itself.

We will start with attack surface reduction or ASR.ASR is about blocking common attack techniques,things like malicious scripts,abuse of office macros,credential theft methods,and living off the land attacks.

Instead of reacting after malware runs,ASR reduces the number of ways an attacker can even get started.

Next,we will focus on Microsoft Defender Firewall.
