95. Intune监控与故障排除门户

在本主题中，我们将聚焦于Intune中最重要的运营工具之一：监控与故障排除门户。

该门户存在的唯一理由，就是速度。

在真实环境中，你很少会批量排查策略问题。

相反，你会收到诸如“我的设备不合规”、“此应用未安装”或“访问被阻止，但我不知道原因”之类的工单。

故障排除门户正是为了快速回答这些问题而设计的。

故障排除门户提供的是以用户为中心的视图。

你不再从策略入手去猜测哪些用户或设备受到了影响，而是直接从用户开始。

由此，Intune会向你展示该用户的设备、分配情况，以及Intune认为当前正在发生什么。

这与传统的故障排除方法相比，是一个重大的转变。

一旦你选中了一位用户，该门户便会汇总关键信号。

你可以立即看到该用户拥有哪些设备、这些设备是否合规、分配了哪些策略，以及设备最后一次签到的时间。

这节省了时间，因为你无需在多个blade之间跳转来构建上下文信息。

该门户对于常见的现实世界问题尤为强大。例如，某个配置配置文件对大多数设备显示为“成功”，但有一位用户报告说未应用。

或者某个应用是必需的，但对特定用户从未安装。

又或者某台设备突然变为不合规状态。

故障排除门户能帮助你隔离问题，判断其是与分配相关、与时间相关，还是特定于设备的问题。

同样重要的是要理解该门户不能做什么。

它不会替你修复问题。

它不能替代终端验证，也无法覆盖策略冲突。

该门户提供的是可见性，而非魔法。

你仍然需要解读数据，并在设备上确认实际行为。

从零信任的角度来看，该门户发挥着至关重要的作用。

零信任要求持续验证。

故障排除门户能让你近乎实时地洞察设备是否仍受信任、是否仍合规，以及是否仍在接收策略。

当访问被阻止或行为发生变化时，该门户往往是理解原因的最快途径。

关键要点很简单。

Intune故障排除门户将猜测转化为调查。

它帮助你从“出问题了”转变为“问题就出在这里”。

在接下来的实验中，我们将亲手使用该门户，按照生产环境中的实际方式，调查真实的设备和策略场景。

英文原文

In this topic,we are going to focus on one of the most important operational tools in Intune,the monitoring and troubleshooting portal.

This portal exists for one reason,and that is speed.

In real environments,you rarely troubleshoot policies in bulk.

Instead,you get tickets like,my device is not compliant,this app did not install,or access is blocked,and I don't know why.

The troubleshooting portal is designed to answer those questions quickly.

The troubleshooting portal is a user-centric view.

Instead of starting from policies and guessing which users or device is affected,you start with the user.

From there,Intune shows you their devices,their assignments,and what Intune believes is happening right now.

This is a major shift from traditional troubleshooting methods.

Once you select a user,the portal aggregates critical signals.

You can immediately see which devices belong to that user,whether those devices are compliant,which policies are assigned,and when the device last checked in.

This saves time because you don't have to jump between multiple blades to build context.

This portal is especially powerful for common real-world problems.For example,a configuration profile shows succeeded for most devices,but one user reports it did not apply.

Or an app is required,but it never installs for a specific user.

Or a device suddenly becomes non-compliant.

The troubleshooting portal helps you isolate whether the issue is assignment-related,timing-related,or device-specific.

It's also important to understand what this portal does not do.

It does not fix problems for you.

It does not replace endpoint validation,and it does not override policy conflicts.

This portal gives you visibility,not magic.

You still need to interpret the data and confirm behavior on the device.

From a zero trust perspective,this portal plays a critical role.

Zero trust requires continuous verification.

The troubleshooting portal gives you near real-time insight into whether a device is still trusted,still compliant,and still receiving policies.

When access is blocked or behavior changes,this portal is often the fastest way to understand why.

The key takeaway is simple.

The Intune troubleshooting portal turns guesswork into investigation.

It helps you move from something is wrong to this is exactly where the problem is.

In the upcoming labs,we will use this portal hands-on to investigate a real device and policy scenario exactly the way you would in production.

96. 解读策略部署与设备健康信号

在本主题中，我们将聚焦于一项技能，它能将自信的Intune操作员与沮丧的操作员区分开来，那就是正确解读策略部署和设备健康信号。

管理员犯下的最大错误之一，就是假设策略状态能说明全部情况。

Intune是异步的，策略不会立即应用，如果你不理解状态值实际代表什么，它们可能会产生误导。

误读信号会导致不必要的故障排除，更糟糕的是，会得出错误的结论。

让我们从你在Intune各处都会看到的四个核心策略状态开始。

“成功”意味着策略已接收并处理成功。

“待处理”意味着设备尚未完成处理。

“失败”意味着策略已处理但遇到错误。

“不适用”意味着该策略根本不适用于此设备。

这些状态中的每一个都讲述着截然不同的故事。

“待处理”是最容易被误解的状态。

“待处理”并不自动意味着出了问题。

它通常意味着设备尚未签入、设备处于离线状态，或者策略正在等待重启或依赖项。

这是正常行为，尤其是在实验室和远程环境中。

在进行故障排除之前，务必先检查时间因素。

现在让我们澄清“失败”与“不适用”的区别。

“失败”意味着Intune尝试应用策略但出了差错。

这可能是设置冲突、不支持的配置或平台限制所致。

“不适用”则不同。

它意味着该策略本就不打算应用。

常见原因包括操作系统错误、设备类型错误，或平台上不存在该设置。

将“不适用”视为错误是一个常见的误区。

仅凭策略状态是不够的。

你必须结合设备健康信号一起解读。

设备健康包括上次签入时间、合规状态、管理权限和主要用户分配。

例如，策略可能是正确的，但设备已有数天未签入，或者设备存在于Entra ID中但未完全由Intune管理。

健康信号提供了上下文背景。

在验证Intune行为时，时间至关重要。

Intune基于签入计划运行。

手动同步可加速测试，但不能保证即时结果。

在同步后，你应始终等待几分钟再得出结论。

耐心是准确故障排除的一部分。

从零信任的角度来看，这些信号直接影响信任。最近未签入的设备可能被视为陈旧设备。

变得不合规的设备可能会失去访问权限。

信任不是永久的。

它是持续评估的。

这就是为什么正确解读信号如此重要。

关键要点如下。

切勿盲目进行故障排除。

务必将策略状态与设备健康状况和时间因素结合起来解读。

当你理解Intune信号实际在告诉你什么时，故障排除就会变得合乎逻辑，而不再令人沮丧。

接下来，我们将进入主题十点三：主动修复与脚本，在那里我们将超越可见性，开始自动修复问题。

英文原文

In this topic,we are going to focus on a skill that separates confident Intune operators from frustrated ones,correctly reading policy deployment and device health signals.

One of the biggest mistakes admins make is assuming that a policy status tells the full story.

Entune is asynchronous,policies don't apply instantly,and status values can be misleading if you don't understand what they actually represent.

Misreading signals leads to unnecessary troubleshooting or worse,incorrect conclusions.

Let's start with the four core policy states you will see everywhere in Intune.

Succeeded means the policy was received and processed successfully.

Pending means the device has not completed processing yet.

Fail means the policy was processed but hit an error.

Not applicable means the policy does not apply to the device at all.

Each of these states tells a very different story.

Pending is the most understood status.

Pending does not automatically mean something is broken.

It usually means the device has not checked in yet,the device is offline,or the policy is waiting for restart or dependency.

This is normal behavior,especially in labs and remote environments.

Before troubleshooting,always check timing.

Now let's clarify fail versus not applicable.

Fail means Entune tries to apply the policy and something went wrong.

This could be a setting conflict,unsupported configuration,or a platform limitation.

Not applicable is different.

It means the policy was never meant to apply.

Common reasons include wrong operating system,wrong device type,or a setting that does not exist on the platform.

Treating not applicable as an error is a common mistake.

Policy status alone is not enough.

You must read device health signals alongside it.

Device health includes last second time,compliance state,management authority,and primary user assignment.

For example,a policy may be correct but the device has not checked in for days or the device exists in EntraID but is not fully managed by Intune.

Health signals provide context.

Timing is critical when validating Intune behavior.

Intune operates on check-in schedules.

Manual sync accelerates testing but it does not guarantee instant results.

You should always allow a few minutes after sync before drawing conclusions.

Patience is part of accurate troubleshooting.

From a zero trust perspective,these signals directly affect trust.A device that has not checked in recently may be considered stale.

A device that becomes non-compliant may lose access.

Trust is not permanent.

It's continuously evaluated.

That's why reading signals correctly matters so much.

The key takeaway is this.

Never troubleshoot blindly.

Always interpret policy status together with device health and timing.

When you understand what intune signals are actually telling you,troubleshooting becomes logical,not frustrating.

Next,we will move into topic 10.3:Proactive remediation and scripts,where we go beyond visibility and start fixing issues automatically.

97. 主动修复与脚本

在本主题中，我们将从观察问题转向使用主动修复来自动解决问题。

传统的故障排除是被动的。

用户报告问题，管理员进行调查，然后逐台设备应用修复。

这种方法无法扩展。

设备会随时间发生漂移，许多问题是可以预测且重复出现的。

主动修复正是为了解决这一问题而存在的。

那么，什么是主动修复？

主动修复是Intune的一项功能，可持续检查设备健康状态，并在检测到问题时自动修复。

它通过两个脚本运行：一个检测脚本和一个修复脚本。

这些脚本按计划运行，无需用户参与。

让我们从检测脚本开始。

检测脚本回答一个简单的问题。

设备是否处于健康状态？

是还是否？

它运行强大的逻辑来检查各种条件，例如服务被禁用、注册表项缺失或安全设置配置错误。

如果脚本报告健康，则不执行任何操作。

如果报告不健康，则触发修复。

修复脚本是执行修复的地方。

此脚本仅在检测失败时运行。

它执行纠正操作，例如重启服务、重置配置或重新启用安全功能。

修复运行后，检测脚本将在下一个周期再次评估设备。

这形成了一个自愈循环。

这在真实环境中非常强大。

常见用例包括重新启用Microsoft Defender功能、修复损坏的更新服务、恢复基于注册表的配置，或纠正由用户或软件引起的设置漂移。

与其产生数百张支持工单，不如让问题在静默中得到修复。

理解主动修复的定位很重要。

它不会取代配置策略。

它也不会取代合规性策略。

相反，它充当运营可靠性层。

策略定义期望状态。

当出现偏差时，修复脚本强制执行该状态。

当设备行为不可预测时，这一点尤其有用。

然而，能力越大，责任也越大。

脚本以提升的权限运行。

编写不当的脚本可能会造成损害。

最佳实践包括彻底测试脚本、避免破坏性操作、保持脚本简洁，并在Intune中监控执行结果。

切勿随意对待修复脚本。

关键要点如下。

主动修复将Intune从被动管理工具转变为主动自愈平台。

如果使用得当，它可以减少事件、提高稳定性并保持设备健康，而且用户往往察觉不到。

英文原文

In this topic,we move from observing problems to fixing them automatically using proactive remediation.

Traditional troubleshooting is reactive.

A user reports an issue,an admin investigates,a fix is applied one device at a time.

This approach does not scale.

Devices drift over time and many issues are predictable and repetitive.

Proactive remediation exists to solve that problem.

So what is proactive remediation?

Proactive remediation is an in-tune feature that continuously checks device health and automatically fixes issues when they are detected.

It works using two scripts,a detection script and a remediation script.

These scripts run on a schedule without user involvement.

Let's start with the detection script.

The detection script answers one simple question.

Is the device in a healthy state?

Yes or no?

It runs powerful logic to check for conditions such as a disabled service,a missing registry key or a misconfigured security settings.

If the script reports healthy,nothing happens.

If it reports unhealthy,remediation is triggered.

The remediation script is where the fix happens.

This script only runs when detection fails.

It performs corrective actions such as restarting a service,resetting a configuration,or re-enabling a security feature.

Once remediation runs,the detection script will evaluate the device again on the next cycle.

This creates a self-healing loop.

This is extremely powerful in real environments.

Common use cases include re-enabling Microsoft Defender features,fixing broken update services,restoring registry-based configurations,or correcting settings drift caused by a user or software.

Instead of hundreds of support tickets,the issue is fixed silently.

It's important to understand where proactive remediation fits.

It does not replace configuration policies.

It does not replace compliance policies.

Instead,it acts as an operational reliability layer.

Policies define desired state.

Remediation scripts enforce it when something slips.

This is especially useful when devices don't behave predictably.

However,with power comes responsibility.

Scripts run with elevated privileges.

Poor written scripts can cause damage.

Best practices include testing scripts thoroughly,avoiding destructive actions,keeping scripts simple,and monitoring execution results in Intune.

Never treat remediation scripts casually.

The key takeaway is this.

Proactive remediation turns Intune from a passive management tool into an active,self-healing platform.

When used correctly,it reduces incidents,improves stability,and keeps devices healthy,often without users ever noticing.

98. 报告与证据导出

在本主题中，我们将聚焦于一个常被忽视的领域：报告与证据。

安全控制措施只有在你能证明其有效运行时才具有意义。

仅仅声称“我们已有策略”或“设备应该合规”是远远不够的。

在真实环境中，你需要向审计员、管理层和安全团队展示证据。这正是Intune报告功能变得至关重要的原因。

Intune提供了多种内置的报告功能。

你可以报告策略部署状态、设备合规性数据、应用安装成功率以及更新态势。

这些报告能回答诸如“策略是否已应用”、“哪些设备失败了”、“问题是孤立的还是普遍存在的”等问题。

知道在哪里找到报告，问题就解决了一半。

大多数日常证据来源于“设备”监控视图、特定策略的状态页面以及“报告”部分。

“终端分析”提供了更深入的洞察，例如性能、启动问题和可靠性趋势。

这些视图既提供了高层概览，也提供了针对具体设备的可见性。

当现有报告不足以满足需求时，你就需要导出证据。

Intune允许你导出报告，通常格式为CSV文件。

这些报告常用于审计提交、安全审查、管理层汇报或事件调查。

导出的数据让你能够保存设备健康状况和策略执行情况在某一特定时间点的快照。

让我们谈谈实际应用场景。

常见的例子包括：证明BitLocker已在所有设备上强制执行、展示随时间变化的合规性趋势、确认应用已成功部署，或演示安全基线已应用。

在事件发生期间，导出的报告往往成为调查记录的一部分。

同样重要的是要理解报告的局限性。

Intune报告是近实时的，而非瞬时的。

有些报告会滞后于设备的实际状态。

数据保留时间是有限的，而且报告往往缺乏关于失败原因的上下文信息。

这就是为什么必须谨慎解读报告，而不能盲目信任。

最佳实践是将报告视为一个流程。

结合多种报告，捕获时间戳，记录范围（例如涉及哪些设备、哪些策略、哪些用户）。

有条理的证据收集能在审计期间节省时间，并防止在安全审查过程中产生混淆。

关键要点是：Intune报告将配置转化为责任。

它是你证明安全控制措施处于活动、有效且已强制执行状态的方式。

在现代环境中，证据与配置同等重要。

在下一个主题中，我们将把所有内容整合起来，探讨利用Intune数据进行端到端故障排除的工作流程。

英文原文

In this topic,we focus on something that often gets overlooked,reporting and evidence.

Security controls only matter if you can prove they are working.

It's not enough to say we have policies or device should be compliant.

In real environments,you are expected to show evidence to auditors,management,and security teams.That's where Intune reporting becomes critical.

Intune provides several built-in reporting capabilities.

You can report on policy deployment status,device compliance date,application installation success,and update posture.

These reports answer questions like did the policy apply,which devices failed,is this issue isolated or widespread.

Knowing where to find reports is half the battle.

Most day-to-day evidence comes from the devices,monitor views,policy-specific status pages,and the report section.

Endpoint analytics adds deeper insight such as performance,startup issues,and reliability trends.

These views give you both high-level and device-specific visibility.

When reporting is not enough,you export evidence.

Intune allows you to export reports usually as CSV files.

These reports are often used for audit submissions,security reviews,management reporting,or incident investigation.

Exported data lets you preserve a point-in-time snapshot of device health and policy enforcement.

Let's talk about real-world use cases.

Common examples include proving BitLocker is enforced across devices,showing compliance trends over time,confirming an application was deployed successfully,or demonstrating that a security baseline is applied.

During incidents,exported reports often become part of the investigation record.

It's also important to understand reporting limitations.

In-tune reporting is near real-time,not instant.

Some reports lag behind actual device state.

Data retention is limited,and reports often lack context on why something failed.

That's why reports should be interpreted carefully,not blindly trusted.

Best practice is to treat reporting as a process.

Combine multiple reports,capture timestamps,document scope,such as which devices,which policies,which users.

Organized evidence collection saves time during audits and prevents confusion during security reviews.

The key takeaway is this.

Intune reporting turns configuration into accountability.

It's how you prove security controls are active,effective,and enforced.

And in modern environments,evidence matters just as much as configuration.

In the next topic,we will bring everything together and look at end-to-end troubleshooting workflows using Intune data.

99. 常见Intune问题及实际解决方案

在本主题中，我们将探讨每位Intune管理员都会遇到的问题。Microsoft Intune出现故障，并非因为Intune本身有问题。

问题往往源于对策略的误解、先决条件缺失，或在缺乏证据的情况下做出了假设。

理解故障原因远比死记硬背操作步骤更有价值。

最常见的抱怨之一是：“我的策略已分配，但未应用。”这通常归结为：分配范围错误、平台不匹配、筛选器排除了设备，或者设备尚未签到。

策略显示“已分配”并不等于“已应用”。

真正的解决方法是：在更改任何内容之前，务必检查设备状态、平台兼容性以及最后签到时间。

另一个经典问题是：设备出现在Entra ID中，却未出现在Intune中。

这不是漏洞。

这是一种误解。

Entra ID的注册或加入并不能保证Intune的注册成功。

必须确保MDM自动注册、正确的许可证以及MDM用户范围全部就位。

真正的解决方法是：验证MDM授权机构，检查用户许可证，并在重新注册设备前确认自动注册范围。

策略冲突会导致一些最难诊断的问题。安全基线、配置配置文件、终端安全策略，它们都可能管理相同的设置。

一旦发生这种情况，行为就会变得不可预测。

某个设置可能显示为已启用，但并未强制执行。

真正的解决方法是：识别重叠部分，减少重复，并避免在多个位置管理同一设置，除非你完全了解优先级规则。

应用失败是另一个主要痛点。

应用失败最常见的原因是：检测规则错误、依赖项未满足，或分配目标指向了错误的对象。

如果检测失败，Intune可能会不断重新安装应用，或永远标记为失败。

真正的解决方法是：首先验证检测逻辑。如果Intune无法确认成功，应用就永远不会处于健康状态。

合规性和访问问题让许多团队感到困惑。设备可能合规却被阻止访问，或不合规却仍被允许访问。

为什么？

因为合规性、Defender风险和条件访问是三种不同的信号，且timing不同。

真正的解决方法是：查看登录日志，而非依赖假设。

登录日志会告诉你访问被允许或被阻止的具体原因。

那么，如何一致地解决Intune问题呢？

请始终遵循以下顺序：范围、平台、分配、设备信号，最后是日志。

切勿盲目更改策略。

务必先验证证据。关键要点是：Intune的问题是可预测的，而可预测的问题就有可重复的解决方法。

成功的Intune管理员不会惊慌失措，他们会进行验证。

他们信任日志、设备状态和证据。正是这种思维模式，区分了被动应对的管理员与自信的Intune工程师。（视频由B站【明文传输不】翻译、配音。如果你喜欢计算机编程教学视频请一键三连，谢谢！）

英文原文

In this topic,we are going to talk about something every Intune admin deals with,that is problems.Microsoft Intune issues don't happen because Intune is broken.

They happen because policies are misunderstood,prerequisites are missing,or assumptions are made without evidence.

Understanding why things break is far more valuable than memorizing clicks.

One of the most common complaints is,my policy is assigned but it's not applying.

This usually comes down to wrong assignment scope,platform mismatch,filters excluding the device or the device simply not checking in yet.

A policy showing assigned does not mean applied.

Real fix always check device status,platform compatibility and last check in time before changing anything.

Another classic issue,the device appears in EntraID but not in Intune.

This is not a bug.

It's a misunderstanding.

EntraID registration or join does not guarantee Intune enrollment.

MDM auto enrollment,correct licensing and MDM user scope must all be in place.

RealFix Verify MDM authority,check the user license and confirm auto enrollment scope before re-enrolling device.

Conflicting policies cause some of the hardest to diagnose problems.Security baselines,configuration profiles,endpoint security policies,they can all manage the same settings.

When that happens,behavior becomes unpredictable.

A setting may appear enabled but not enforced.

Real fix identity overlap,reduce duplication and avoid managing the same setting in multiple places unless you fully understand precedence.

Application failures are another major pain point.

Apps fail most often because detection rules are wrong,dependencies are not met or assignments target the wrong ideas.

If detection fails,Intune may keep reinstalling the app or market fail forever.

Real fix validate detection logic first.If Intune cannot confirm success,the app will never be healthy.

Compliance and access issues confuse many teams.A device can be compliant but still blocked or non-compliant but still allowed.

Why?

Because compliance,defender risk and conditional access are separate signals with different timing.

The real fix of this is check sign-in logs,not assumptions.

Sign-in logs tells you why access was allowed or blocked.

So how do you fix Intune issues consistently?

Always follow this order.Scope,then platform,then assignment,then device signals and then logs.

Never change policies blindly.

Always validate evidence first.The key takeaway is this.

Intune problems are predictable and predictable problems have repeatable fixes.

Admins who succeeded with Intune don't panic,they validate.

They trust logs,device status,and evidence.That mindset is what separates reactive admins from confident in-tune engineers.

100. 总结

让我们总结第十部分。

在本部分中，你学会了如何看清Intune实际在做什么，而非你认为它在做什么。

你探索了那些能将猜测与证据区分开来的监控和故障排除工具。

你学会了如何解读策略部署状态、设备健康信号和日志，并理解它们所传达的信息。

本部分最重要的思维转变是：分配并不等于强制执行，策略存在并不意味着它已应用，绿色复选标记也并不总是代表成功。

你学会了信任日志、设备状态和登录数据，而非依赖假设。

你还看到Intune的问题并非随机出现，它们遵循一定的模式：范围问题、注册缺口、冲突、检测失败以及时间延迟。

正因为问题是可预测的，所以修复方法也是可重复的。

本部分是将Intune从配置工具转变为运营平台的关键。

它不仅赋予你部署策略的信心，更让你能够捍卫自己的决策、排除故障并清晰地解释结果。

掌握了监控和故障排除后，你现在已准备好继续前进，因为你能够证明Intune的实际行为，并在其表现不符合预期时加以修复。

英文原文

Let's wrap up section 10.

In this section,you learn how to see what Intune is actually doing,not what you think it's doing.

You explore the monitoring and troubleshooting tools that separate guesswork from evidence.

You learn how to read policy deployment status,device health signals and logs,and how to understand what they are telling you.

The most important mindset shift in this section is this.

Assignment does not mean enforcement,a policy existing does not mean it's applied,and a green check mark does not always mean success.

You learn to trust logs,device status,and sign in data instead of assumptions.

You also saw the Intune problems are not random,they follow patterns,scope issues,enrollment gaps,conflicts,detection failures,and timing delays.

And because the problems are predictable,the fixes are repeatable.

This section is what turns Intune from a configuration tool into an operational platform.

It gives you confidence not just to deploy policies,but to defend your decisions,troubleshoot issues,and explain outcomes clearly.

With monitoring and troubleshooting mastered,you are now ready to move forward knowing you can prove what Intune is doing and fix it when it does not behave as expected.

101. 实验10-1：使用Intune故障排除门户

欢迎进入实验十点一。

使用Intune故障排除门户。

在本实验中，我们将使用Microsoft Intune中最强大却最常被低估的工具之一：“故障排除加支持”门户。

大多数Intune管理员从策略侧进行故障排除。

他们会问这样的问题：策略已分配了吗？

配置存在吗？

但现实世界中的问题并非如此报告。

在生产环境中，问题是以用户为中心的。

用户会说：我的应用未安装、我的设备突然变为不合规，或我无法访问公司资源。

故障排除门户彻底扭转了这一思维模式。

我们不再从策略入手，而是从用户开始，然后向外扩展到设备、应用、合规性和分配情况。

这正是经验丰富的Intune管理员快速调查问题、无需猜测或点击十几个不同页面的方法。

在本实验中，你将学习如何从用户视角调查问题，判断问题是出在用户、设备、策略还是时间因素上，并迅速识别为何某些行为与预期不符。

这不是一个理论实验。

这是Intune在真实环境中进行故障排除的实际方式。

那么，让我们开始吧。

好的，首先我们需要进入Intune中的“故障排除加支持”区域。

简单提醒一下我们为何从这里开始。

该门户专为从用户体验角度进行故障排除而构建。

因此，我们不必先费力查找策略，而是直接进入Intune汇总跨设备、应用、合规性和策略状态信号的地方，所有这些都关联回特定用户。

现在屏幕上，我位于Intune管理中心，点击“故障排除加支持”。

请稍等几秒让其加载。

有时此页面需要一点时间，因为它正在后台从多个服务拉取数据。

你在此处应看到的是登录页面，顶部有一个“选择用户”的搜索框。

这是关键细节。

此工具以用户为先。如果你看不到“故障排除加支持”选项，通常是由于以下三种情况之一。

第一，你的账户没有正确的Intune角色权限。

第二，你位于错误的租户中，或者门户用户界面尚未完全刷新。

但在正常的实验设置中，你应该能进入此页面，并看到准备好的用户搜索字段。

既然我们已位于“故障排除加支持”登录页面，下一步是选择一位用户。

这是此门户中的思维转变。

我们不是在孤立地排查某个策略。

我们是在排查用户的体验。

因为在现实生活中，工单不会说“策略失败”。

它们会说“我的应用未安装”、“我无法访问电子邮件”或“我的设备不合规”。

因此在屏幕上，我正点击“选择用户”框，现在开始输入你的实验用户：test user。

当你输入时，Intune会从Entra ID建议匹配的用户。

一旦看到test user出现，点击该名称以选中它。

此时，请暂停几秒钟，因为门户现在正在加载以用户为中心的配置文件视图。

它正在整合多项内容。

既然我们已选中测试用户，请注意Intune立即执行的操作。

在屏幕顶部，我们可以看到用户身份、用户名、电子邮件地址和用户主体名称。

在右侧，我们还看到用户状态，确认该账户已启用并获得适当许可。

这已经告诉我们一些重要信息。

如果账户被禁用或未获许可，我们稍后调查的许多问题其实早已有了答案。

现在让我们进入“摘要”选项卡。

这是故障排除门户的默认登录视图。

此页面提供了用户整个Intune体验的高级健康快照。

从左侧开始，我们看到策略状态。

此处，Intune告诉我们有多少策略处于合规、冲突、待定或不适用状态。

这里的冲突常常解释了设备上的不一致行为，尤其是当多个配置文件针对相同设置时。

现在，看看合规性。

这显示了用户设备当前是否满足合规性要求。

此处的一个错误随后可能直接导致通过条件访问被阻止访问。

而在左侧，我们有应用程序部分。

此部分告诉我们从用户视角实际发生了什么，而非我们预期会发生什么。

已安装、失败、可用或等待中，这往往是解决应用相关工单的关键所在。

接下来是应用保护。

显示受保护应用的签到和同步行为。

如果应用未签到，策略执行可能不会按预期发生。

最后，查看设备。

我们可以立即看到有多少设备已注册，是否有任何设备离线、不合规或存在注册失败。

此处的注册失败往往解释了为何其他一切均未生效。

此步骤的关键教学点很简单。

在点击任何策略或设备之前，此摘要已告诉我们下一步该调查哪里。

一旦你理解此页面，故障排除便成为有guided的过程，而非盲目猜测。

既然我们理解了用户摘要，下一步是查看与该用户关联的设备。

因为策略和应用并非直接应用于用户，而是应用于设备。

从摘要页面，将光标移至设备部分。

此处，Intune立即告诉我们该用户有多少设备已注册，以及其中是否有任何设备离线、禁用、不合规或存在注册失败。

这一点很重要，因为如果错误的设备设为主设备，或设备最近未签到，那么即使分配看起来完美，下游的一切也可能失败。

在此暂停，查看列：设备名称、操作系统、合规状态和最后签到时间。

最后签到时间是Intune问题最常见的根本原因之一。

如果设备最近未签到，策略可能显示为待定或未应用，即使实际上没有任何故障。

现在我们从用户摘要进入“设备”选项卡，因为这里是Intune告诉我们哪些终端实际与该用户关联的地方。

在顶部，注意我们仍限定在test user零一。

现在我们正在查看设备视图。

此表格显示了Intune认为属于该用户的每台设备。

从左侧开始，查看名称列。

我们在此可以看到三种不同的设备类型：一台Android工作配置文件设备、一台Windows十企业设备和一台Windows十一企业设备。

这立即告诉我们一个重要信息。

该用户跨多个平台工作，因此行为可能因操作系统而异。

接下来，查看“管理者”。

所有这些设备均由Intune管理，这确认它们已正确注册。

如果某设备由其他东西管理或未被管理，那就能解释为何策略未应用。

接下来，检查所有权。

我们可以看到个人设备和企业设备并存。

所有权影响哪些策略适用，尤其是对于合规性、配置策略和条件访问。

继续向右，查看Intune合规性和Microsoft Entra合规性。

两者在此均显示为合规，意味着Intune和Entra对设备健康状况达成一致。

如果这两者出现分歧，访问问题往往会随之而来。

接下来是应用生命周期状态。

状态为成功，告诉我们应用处理已完成且无错误。

如果应用失败或卡住，此列会立即突显出来。

现在查看操作系统和操作系统版本。

这很重要，因为许多策略是针对特定操作系统的。

Windows十策略不会以相同方式应用于Windows十一，而Android的行为又有所不同。

最后，请密切关注最后签到时间。

这些时间戳告诉我们Intune最后一次收到设备信号的时间。

过时的签到是策略显示为待定或缺失的最常见原因之一。

因此，关键教训是：在排查策略或应用之前，先确认正确的设备、正确的所有权以及最近的签到。

让我们转到“策略”选项卡。

既然我们已确认用户和设备，下一步是了解Intune实际针对该用户定位了哪些内容。

此视图显示了分配给该用户的每项策略，无论他们使用的是哪台设备。

注意此处的关键列。

首先，查看名称列。

这些是真实的生产风格策略：基线、合规性策略、Defender加入、限制和更新环。

接下来，查看目标。

所有这些都定向到用户，而非直接定向到设备。

现在检查分配情况。

此处所有内容均为“包含”，这告诉我们该用户在范围内。

如果某些内容未应用，此列会立即告诉我们该用户是被排除还是从未被定向。

查看类型和平台。

你可以看到设置目录、合规性、配置策略和终端安全策略的混合，并且其范围限定为Windows十及更高版本。

这很重要，因为这些策略不会应用于Android或其他平台，即使用户拥有这些设备。

因此，关键教训是：策略问题往往并非失败，而是范围或平台不匹配。

现在，策略定义意图，而应用定义体验。

让我们转向应用程序。

此处我们确切看到哪些应用分配给了该用户。

屏幕上我们有来自Microsoft Store的7zip win32和Microsoft To Do。

查看应用所针对的设备。

这告诉我们该用户的多少设备实际上有资格接收该应用。

然后检查“已分配”。

同样，两者均为“包含”，因此分配不是问题所在。

这是一个关键的故障排除点。

如果用户说“我的应用未安装”，此视图会立即告诉我们：应用是否已分配？设备是否有资格？平台是否正确？

因此，只需几次点击，我们就从策略意图走向了应用现实。

你会注意到此处还有其他几个可用选项卡，如组、应用保护策略、更新、注册限制和诊断。

这些选项卡功能强大，但具有情境性。

你不应该从它们开始。

在真实的故障排除中，你应从摘要开始，确认设备，验证策略意图，然后检查应用行为。

一旦你缩小了问题范围，再深入高级选项卡。

因此，在本实验中，你按照设计初衷使用了Intune故障排除门户：从用户开始，而非从策略开始。

你学会了如何阅读用户摘要以快速评估整体健康状况，识别哪些设备实际与该用户关联，验证策略目标和范围，并确认应用分配和平台资格。

更重要的是，你学习了一种真实的故障排除方法。

从用户体验开始，缩小到设备，然后验证策略和应用行为。

此门户本身并不能修复问题，但它消除了猜测，并告诉你下一步该调查哪里。

英文原文

Welcome to lab 10.1.

Use Intune Troubleshooting Portal.

In this lab,we are going to work with one of the most powerful and most underused tool in Microsoft Intune,the Troubleshooting Plus Support Portal.

Most Intune admins troubleshoot from the policy side.

They ask questions like,is the policy assigned?

Does the configuration exist?

But that's not how real-world issues are reported.

In production,problems are user-centric.

A user says,my app did not install,my device suddenly became non-compliant,or I cannot access company resources.

The troubleshooting portal flips the entire mindset.

Instead of starting with policies,we start with the user and then work outward to devices,apps,compliance,and assignments.

This is how experienced Intune admins investigate issues quickly without guessing or clicking through 10 different plates.

In this lab,you will learn how to investigate issues from the user's perspective,understand whether the problem is user,device,policy,or timing,and quickly identify why something behaved differently than expected.

This is not a theory lab.

This is how Intune is actually troubleshooting in real environments.

So let's get started.

Alright,first thing,we need to get to the troubleshooting plus support area in Intune.

A quick reminder of why we are starting here.

This portal is built to troubleshoot from the user experience angle.

So instead of hunting through policies first,we come to the place where Intune aggregates signals across devices,apps,compliance and policy status,all tied back to a user.

Now on screen,I'm on Intune Admin Center and click on Troubleshoot plus Support.

Give it few seconds to load.

Sometimes this page takes a moment because it's pulling data from multiple services in the background.

What you should see here is the landing page with a select user search box at the top.

That's the key details.

This tool is user first.If you don't see the troubleshooting plus support option,that's usually one of three things.

First,your account does not have the right Intune role permissions.

Second,you are in the wrong tenant or the portal user interface has not fully refreshed.

But in a normal lab setup,you should land on this page with the user search field ready.

Now that we are on the troubleshooting plus support landing page,the next step is to select a user.

This is the mindset shift in this portal.

We are not troubleshooting a policy in isolation.

We are troubleshooting the user's experience.

Because in real life,tickets don't say policy failed.

They say my app did not install or I cannot access email or my device is non-compliant.

So on the screen,I'm clicking into the select user box and now start typing your lab user,test user.

As you type in,Intune will suggest matching users from EntraID.

Once you see test user appear,click the name to select it.

At this point,pause for a few seconds because the portal now loads a user-centric profile view.

It's pulling multiple things together.

Now that we have selected our test user,notice what Intune does immediately.

At the top of the screen,we can see the user identity,the username,email address,and user principal name.

On the right,we also see user status confirming that the account is enabled and properly licensed.

This already tells us something important.

If the account were disabled or unlicensed,many issues we investigate later would already be explained.

Now let's move into the summary tab.

This is the default landing view for the troubleshooting portal.

This page is a high-level health snapshot of the user's entire Intune experience.

Starting on the left,we see policy status.

Here,Intune tells us how many policies are compliant in conflict,pending,or not applicable.

Conflicts here often explain inconsistent behavior on the device,especially when multiple profiles target the same settings.

Now,look at the compliance.

This shows whether the user device meets compliance requirement right now.

A single error here can later translate directly into blocked access through conditional access.

And on the left,we have applications.

This section tells us what actually happened from the user's perspective,not what we expected to happen.

Installed,failed,available,or waiting,this is often where app-related tickets are solved.

Next,we have app protection.

Showing check-in and sync behavior for protected apps.

If apps are not checking in,policy enforcement may not occur as expected.

Finally,look at devices.

We can immediately see how many devices are enrolled,whether any or offline,non-compliant,or have enrollment failures.

Enrollment failures here often explain why nothing else worked.

The key teaching point for this step is simple.

Before clicking into any policy or device,this summary already tells us where to investigate next.

Once you understand this page,troubleshooting becomes guided,not guesswork.

Now that we understand the user summary,the next step is to look at the devices tied to this user.

Because policies and apps don't apply to users directly,they apply to devices.

From the summary page,move your cursor to the devices section.

Here,Intune immediately tells us how many devices are enrolled for this user and whether any of them are offline,disabled,non-compliant,or have enrollment failures.

This is important because if the wrong device is primary or the device has not checked in recently,everything else downstream can fail,even if assignment looks perfect.

Pause here and look at the columns Device's name,Operating Systems,Compliance Status and the Last Check-In Time.

The last check-in time is one of the most common root causes of Intune issues.

If a device has not checked in recently,policies may show as pending or not applied,even though nothing is actually broken.

Now we move from the user summary into the devices tab because this is where Intune tells us which endpoints are actually associated with the user.

At the top,notice we are still scoped to test user 01.

And now we are looking at the devices view.

This table shows every device Intune believes belongs to this user.

Starting from the left,look at the name column.

We can see three different devices types here:an Android work profile device,a Windows 10 corporate device,and a Windows 11 corporate device.

Immediately,this tells us something important.

This user works across multiple platforms,so behavior may differ by operating system.

Next,look at managed by.

All of these devices are managed by Intune,which confirms they are properly enrolled.

If a device were managed by something else or unmanaged,that would explain why a policy did not apply.

Next,check ownership.

We can see both personal and corporate devices.

Ownership affects which policies apply,especially for compliance,configuration policies,and conditional access.

Moving across,look at Intune Compliance and Microsoft Entra compliance.

Both show compliant here,meaning Intune and Entra agree on device health.

If these ever disagree,access issues often follow.

Next is app lifecycle status.

A status of success tells us app processing completed without errors.

If apps were failing or stuck,this column would immediately highlight it.

Now look at the operating system and operating system version.

This matters because many policies are operating system specific.

A Windows 10 policy won't apply the same way to Windows 11,and Android behaves differently again.

Finally,pay close attention to last check-in.

These timestamps tell us when Intune last heard from the device.

A stale check-in is one of the most common reasons policies appear pending or missing.

So the key lesson is this.Before troubleshooting a policy or an app,confirm the right device,the right ownership,and a recent check-in.

Let's move to the policy tab.

Now that we confirm that user and devices,the next step is to understand what Intune is actually targeting this user with.

This view shows every policy assigned to this user,regardless of which device they are using.

Notice the key column here.

First,look at name column.

These are real production,these are real production style policies,baselines,compliance policy,defender onboarding,restrictions,and update rings.

Next,look at targeting.

All of these are targeted to the user,not directly to the device.

Now check the assignment.

Everything here is included which tells us the user is in scope.

If something was not applying,this column would immediately tell us whether the user was excluded or never targeted.

Look at the type and platform.

You can see a mix of settings catalog,compliance,configuration policies,and endpoint security policies and their scope to Windows 10 and later.

That matters because these policies won't apply to Android or other platforms even though the user has those devices.

So the key lesson is policy issues are often not failures,their scope or platform mismatches.

Now policies define intent,but apps define experience.

Let's move to applications.

Here we see exactly which apps are assigned to this user.

On screen we have 7zip win32 and Microsoft To Do from the Microsoft Store.

Look at the devices targeted with app.

This tells us how many of the user devices are actually eligible to receive the app.

Then check assigned.

Again both are included so assignment is not the issue.

This is a critical troubleshooting point.

If a user says my app did not install,this view immediately tells us was the app assigned,was the device eligible or was the platform correct?

So in just a couple of clicks,we have gone from policy intent to application reality.

You will notice there are several other tabs available here like groups,app protection policy,updates,enrollment restrictions and diagnostics.

These tabs are powerful but they are situational.

You don't start with them.

In real troubleshooting,you begin with the summary,confirm the device,validate policy intent and check application behavior.

Once you have narrowed down the problem,then you go deeper into the advanced tabs.

So in this lab,you use the Intune troubleshooting portal the way it's designed to be used,starting with the user,not the policy.

You learn how to read the user summary to quickly assess overall health,identify which devices are actually associated with the user,validate policy targeting and scope,and confirm application assignments and platform eligibility.

More importantly,you learn a real troubleshooting approach.

Start with the user experience,narrow down to the device,and then validate policy and app behavior.

This portal does not fix issues by itself,but it removes guesswork and shows you where to investigate next.

102. 实验10-2：导出Intune管理报告

欢迎进入实验十点二：导出Intune管理报告。

在本实验中，我们将重点从配置转向可见性与证据。

到目前为止，我们已配置了策略、强制执行了合规性、保护了设备并验证了行为。

但在真实环境中，除非你能证明这些工作已完成，否则它们就不算真正结束。

Intune管理员经常会被问到这样的问题：目前哪些设备是合规的？

哪些策略失败了？发生在哪些设备上？

你能否为审计、安全审查或管理层提供证据？

仪表板对日常监控很有用，但还远远不够。

审计员、安全团队和领导层需要的是可导出的数据，以便他们日后审查、归档和参考。

这正是Intune报告功能的价值所在。

在本实验中，你将学习如何浏览Intune内置的报告区域、生成有意义的报告，并将其导出为CSV格式。

更重要的是，你将了解这些导出的报告实际上如何用于审计、合规性证据、故障排除文档以及高管汇报。

本实验的重点不是简单地点击浏览图表。

而是要将Intune数据转化为可辩护的安全态势证明。

那么，让我们开始吧。

好的，第一步是进入Intune报告中心，因为这里是Intune将配置转化为证据的地方。

因此，请打开浏览器，导航至Intune管理中心，然后点击“报告”。

现在请稍停片刻，等待报告页面完全加载，因为该区域与“设备”或“终端安全”不同。

这是一个中心位置，Intune在此汇总整个环境的报告数据。

进入报告页面后，先不要点击任何内容。

只需快速浏览一下显示的主要部分。

你应该能看到诸如“设备合规性”、“设备配置”和“终端安全”等类别。

这些类别很重要，因为它们代表了真实组织中最常见的报告需求。

例如，“设备合规性”是审计和条件访问所依赖的内容。

“设备配置”可帮助你证明策略已成功应用。

而“终端安全”则是安全运营团队通常要求查看的内容。因此，目标很简单。

成功进入报告区域并识别主要报告类别即可。

现在，让我们点击“设备合规性”。

进入“设备合规性”后，查找名为“报告”的选项。

这一点很重要，因为Intune虽有仪表板，但“报告”视图才是你获取结构化、可导出报告的地方。

现在打开“设备合规性报告”。

既然我们已进入“设备合规性报告”页面，这里就是Intune生成可供导出的合规性数据的地方。

在屏幕顶部，注意有一条消息提示：报告生成可能需要一些时间，并在七十二小时后过期。

这是第一个线索，表明这不仅仅是一个仪表板。

这是一份专为证据和导出而生成的报告。

现在让我们看看筛选器。

默认情况下，“合规状态”、“操作系统”、“Microsoft Managed Desktop”和“所有权”均设置为“全部”。

对于本实验，我们将保持这些设置不变，以便获得整个环境的完整合规性概览。

在真实环境中，这些筛选器常用于回答具体问题。

例如，仅显示不合规的设备，或仅显示Windows终端。

现在筛选器已设置好，点击“生成报告”。

请稍等片刻。

Intune正在查询租户中所有设备的实时数据。

报告生成完毕后，下方的表格将填充数据。

当表格出现时，你将看到诸如“设备名称”、“主UPN”、“合规状态”、“操作系统及版本”和“所有权”等列。

这个表格回答了一个非常常见的审计问题。

目前哪些设备是合规的？它们分配给了谁？

如果表格为空或加载缓慢，这并不是错误。

通常这意味着报告仍在生成中，或者环境规模较小。

因此，在此阶段先不要导出。

既然合规性报告已生成且设备数据可见，此时报告才成为真正的证据。

仪表板有助于监控，但在企业环境中，审计员、安全团队和管理层不会只看仪表板，他们会要求导出的数据。

因此，在此合规性报告视图中，将光标移至顶部并点击“导出”。

当你点击“导出”时，系统会提示：这将把所有选定的列和行（含已应用的筛选器）导出为压缩的逗号分隔值文件，即CSV文件。

是否继续？

我将点击“是”。

当你点击“导出”后，Intune会根据你刚才生成的筛选器和数据准备报告以下载。

根据环境规模大小，这可能需要几秒钟，在较大的租户中则可能稍长一些。

下载完成后，打开它，右键点击并选择“解压”。

解压。

打开下载的文件。

我将展开各列。

查看CSV文件时，你应该能认出与门户中看到相同的数据。

包括设备名称、UPN、合规状态、操作系统详细信息以及所有权信息。

这一点很重要，因为时间戳显示了数据何时有效，这对审计和调查至关重要。

这里的关键要点是：如果未导出，就不构成证据。

此文件现在可以分享给审计员、附加到工单上，或作为特定时间点合规性的证明进行归档。

既然我们已导出了合规性证据，组织通常会问的下一个问题是：我们的配置策略实际交付到了哪些设备？

这正是“配置部署报告”所回答的问题。

我将关闭CSV文件并导航回你的Intune管理中心。

返回“报告”。

点击“设备配置”。

现在让我们点击“报告”选项卡，然后进入“策略配置状态”。

目前表格为空，因为报告尚未生成。

因此，我们要做的第一件事是点击“生成报告”。

点击“生成报告”后，请稍停片刻。

Intune正在汇总所有目标设备的配置部署数据。

报告生成完毕后，页面将填充数据。

你将开始看到“顶级配置策略状态”下列出的配置策略，以及显示策略名称、策略类型、操作系统、成功设备数量、错误设备数量以及是否存在冲突的列。

此视图极其重要，因为它能立即突显哪些策略运行正常，哪些需要关注。

既然数据已可见，这正是组织用来证明配置意图确实转化为设备强制执行的内容。

从这里，你可以使用“列”调整可见字段，或继续导出数据以作文档记录。

从这里，你可以使用“列”调整可见字段，或继续导出数据以作文档记录。

要将其转化为证据，请点击“导出”并将报告下载为CSV文件。

点击“是”。

该CSV文件提供了设备级别的部署结果，显示成功、错误和冲突状态，可与审计员分享、附加到工单或存储为合规性证据。

报告生成并导出后，点击打开。

我将打开CSV文件。

我将展开各列。

此导出的CSV展示了大规模的配置策略结果。

对于每项策略，有多少设备是合规的、不合规的或处于冲突状态？

这正是审计员和安全团队用来验证策略执行情况并确定何处需要跟进的格式。

让我们关闭CSV文件并返回Intune管理中心。

现在我们将导出终端安全报告，因为这是将Intune与安全运营连接起来的报告区域。

当SOC团队提出诸如“终端是否受到保护？”或“我们是否有Defender覆盖范围和强制执行？”等问题时，Intune正是在此处提供此类海报级证据。

因此，让我们返回“报告”。

在“终端安全”下，点击“Microsoft Defender防病毒”。

现在我们从Microsoft Defender防病毒开始，进入终端安全报告部分。

屏幕上，我们位于Microsoft Defender防病毒界面，默认停留在“摘要”选项卡。

让我们转到“报告”，然后点击“防病毒代理状态”。

现在我们查看的是Microsoft Defender防病毒代理状态报告，这正是Intune报告直接服务于安全运营的关键点。

在页面顶部，我们可以看到“导出”选项。

这才是SOC团队和审计员真正关心的内容。

在导出之前，花点时间理解此报告显示的内容。

在摘要栏中，我们可以看到跨设备的整体防病毒状态：有多少设备是干净的，是否有任何设备正在等待扫描或重启，以及是否有任何设备处于严重状态。

在其下方，我们获得设备级别的详细信息，例如设备名称、设备状态、管理者、严重故障等。

这正是安全团队在验证终端保护时所期望的详细程度。

现在要将其转化为证据，请点击“导出”，选择CSV，并等待下载完成。

下载完成后，打开CSV文件，确认其中包含你在此门户中看到的相同设备级别字段。

此导出文件现在可以分享给SOC团队、附加到审计文档中，或作为特定时间点终端安全态势的证明进行存储。

既然Defender防病毒报告已导出，最后一步是学习如何正确解读这些数据。

从左侧开始，查看设备名称和设备状态。

这确认了记录所属的终端，以及Defender当前报告该设备是干净还是处于其他状态。

接下来，查看“管理者”和“反恶意软件版本”。

这告诉我们该设备由Intune管理，并确认了终端上运行的Defender引擎版本。

这在验证跨设备一致性时非常有用。

现在转向“严重故障”和“产品状态”。

“严重故障”值为假，表示Defender运行正常。

“产品状态”提供了Defender内部健康状态的数字表示，SOC团队用它来确认保护功能处于活动状态。

继续向下滚动，查看“篡改保护已启用”和“实时保护已启用”。

这些是关键的安全控制项。

如果其中任何一项为假，则立即表明终端保护被削弱。

现在请注意“最后报告日期时间”以及与扫描相关的时间戳。

这些字段告诉你设备最后一次报告安全数据的时间。

过时的时间戳是发现那些看似健康但最近未签入设备的最快方法之一。

最后，滚动到最右侧，查看UPN、用户电子邮件和用户名。

这是报告生成时与该设备关联的用户上下文。

在Defender报告中，用户数据是次要的。

设备始终是主要的安全对象。

这里的关键要点是：此CSP不仅显示Defender存在，它还证明了它在哪些设备上运行得有多好，以及上次确认状态的时间。

一旦你能自信地阅读此报告，便可将其用于SOC审查、审计和事件调查。

在本实验中，你学习了如何将Intune数据转化为真实、可辩护的证据。

你浏览了Intune的报告区域，并生成了涵盖设备合规性、配置策略部署以及带有Microsoft Defender的终端安全的报告。

更重要的是，你不仅仅是导出了报告，还学会了如何正确解读它们。

你看到了合规性报告如何回答设备是否满足要求，配置报告如何证明策略确实已交付，以及Defender报告如何展示设备级别的真实安全态势。

通过审查导出的CSV文件，你学会了如何识别健康设备与风险设备、过时或未报告的终端，以及已启用、禁用或待操作的安全控制项。

这就是简单管理Intune与专业运营Intune之间的区别。

优秀的Intune管理员不会仅依赖仪表板。

他们能够利用数据为审计提供证据、支持SOC调查，并向领导层解释安全态势。

至此，报告实验完成，本节也告一段落。

英文原文

Welcome to lab 10.2 Export an Intune Management Report.

In this lab,we shift focus from configuration to visibility and evidence.

Up to this point,we have configured policies,enforced compliance,secure devices,and validated behavior.

But in real environments,that work is not considered complete unless you can prove it.

Intune administrators are regularly asked questions like,which devices are compliant right now?

Which policies failed and on which devices?

Can you provide evidence for an audit,a security review,or management?

Dashboards are useful for day-to-day monitoring,but they are not enough.

Auditors,security teams,and leadership want exportable data they can review,archive,and reference later.

That's where Intune reporting comes in.

In this lab,you will learn how to navigate Intune's built-in reporting areas,generate meaningful reports,and export them in CSV format.

More importantly,you will learn how those exported reports are actually used for audits,compliance evidence,troubleshooting documentation,and executive reporting.

This lab is not about clicking through charts.

It's about turning Intune data into defensible proof of security posture.

So let's begin.

Alright,first step is about getting into the Intune reporting hub because this is where Intune turns configuration into evidence.

So open up the browser,navigate to Intune Admin Center and click on Reports.

Now pause for a second and let the reports page load fully because this area is different from devices or endpoint security.

It's a central location where Intune aggregates reporting across the environment.

Once you are in reports,don't click anything yet.

Just take a quick look at the main section that appear.

You should see categories such as device compliance,device configuration and endpoint security.

These categories matter because they represent the most common reporting asks in real organizations.

For example,device compliance is what audits and conditional access depend on.

Device configuration helps you to prove policies applied successfully.

And endpoint security is what security operation teams usually request.So the goal is simple.

You can successfully reach the reports area and identify the main reporting categories.

Now let's click on device compliance.

Once you are in device compliance,look for the option called reports.

This is important because Intune has dashboards but the reports view is where you get structured exportable reporting.

Now open the device compliance report.

Now that we are inside device compliance report,this page is where Intune generates export ready compliance data.

At the top of the screen,notice the message is saying the report may take some time to generate and will expire after 72 hours.

That's your first clue that this is not just a dashboard.

This is a generated report designed for evidence and export.

Now let's look at the filters.

By default,compliance status,operating system,Microsoft Managed Desktop and ownership are all set to all.

For this lab,we will leave them as is so we get a complete compliance picture across the environment.

In real environments,these filters are often used to answer specific questions.

For example,showing only non-compliant devices or only Windows endpoints.

Now with the filter set,click generate report.

Pause for a moment.

Intune is now querying live device data across the tenant.

Once the report finishes generating,the table below will populate.

When it appears,you will see columns such as device name,primary UPN,compliance status,operating system and operating version,and ownership.

This table answers a very common audit question.

Which devices are compliant right now and who are they assigned to?

If the table is empty or slow to load,that's not an error.

It usually means the report is still generating or the environment is small.

So at this stage,don't export yet.

Now that the compliance report has been generated and the device data is visible,this is where the report becomes real evidence.

Dashboards are helpful for monitoring,but in enterprise environments,auditors,security teams and management don't look at dashboards,they ask for exported data.

So from this compliance report view,move your cursors to top and click export.

When you click export,so it says this will export all selected columns and rows with filters applied to compressed comma separated values,which is.csv file.

Do you want to continue?

I'll click yes.

When you click export,Intune prepares the report for download using the filters and data you just generated.

Depending on the size of the environment,this can take a few seconds or a bit longer in a larger tenants.

Once downloaded,open it,right-click and click to extract.

Extract.

Open the downloaded file.

I'll expand the columns.

When you look at the CSV,you should recognize the same data you saw in the portal.

Device name,UPN,compliance status,operating system details,and ownership information.

This is important because timestamps show when the data was valid,which matters for audits and investigations.

The key takeaway here is this.

If it's not exported,it's not an evidence.

This file can now be shared with auditors,attached to a ticket or archived as proof of compliance at a specific point in time.

Now that we have exported compliance evidence,the next question organizations usually ask is,what our configuration policies actually delivered to devices?

That's what configuration deployment reports answer.

I'll close the CSV file and navigate back to your Intune admin center.

Go back to reports.

Click on device configurations.

Now let's click on reports tab and go to policy configuration status.

Right now the table is empty because the report has not been generated yet.

So the first thing we do is click generate report.

After clicking generate report,pause for a moment.

Intune is now aggregating configuration deployment data across all targeted devices.

Once the report finishes generating,the page will populate with the data.

You will start to see configuration policies listed under top configuration policy status along with columns showing policy name,policy type,operating system,how many devices were successful,how many have errors,whether there are any conflicts.

This view is extremely important because it immediately highlights which policies are healthy and which one needs attention.

Now that the data is visible,this is what organizations use to prove that configuration intent actually translated into device enforcement.

From here,you can adjust visible fields using columns or proceed to export the data for documentation.

From here,you can adjust visible fields using columns or proceed to export the data for documentation.

To turn this into evidence,click export and download the report as CSV file.

Click yes.

That CSV provides device level deployment results showing success,error and conflict states which can be shared with auditors,attached to tickets or stored as compliance evidence.

Once the report has been generated and exported,click to open.

I'll open the CSV file.

I'll expand the columns.

This exported CSV shows configuration policy results at scale.

How many devices are compliant,non-compliant or in conflict for each policy?

This is the exact format auditors and security teams use to validate policy enforcement and identify where follow-up is needed.

Let's close the CSV file and go back to the Intune Admin Center.

Now we will export an endpoint security report because this is the reporting area that connects Intune to security operations.

When SOC teams ask questions like are endpoints protected or do we have defender coverage and enforcement?

This is where Intune provides that poster evidence.

So let's go back to reports.

Under the endpoint security,click on Microsoft Defender Antivirus.

Now we move into endpoint security reporting starting with Microsoft Defender Antivirus.

On screen,we are in the Microsoft Defender Antivirus and by default we land on the summary tab.

Let's go to reports and then click on antivirus agent status.

Now we are looking at the Microsoft Defender Antivirus Antivirus Agent Status Report and this is the point where Intune reporting becomes directly useful for security operations.

At the top of the page we can see the export option.

This is what SOC teams and auditors actually care about.

Before exporting,take a moment to understand what this report is showing.

In the summary bar,we can see the overall antivirus state across devices,how many devices are clean,whether any are waiting for scans or reboots and whether any devices are in critical state.

Below that,we get device level details such as device name,device state,managed by,critical failure and so on.

This is exactly the level of detail security team expect when validating endpoint protection.

Now to run this into evidence,click export,choose CSV and wait for the download to complete.

Once downloaded,open the CSV file and confirm it contains the same device level fields you see here in the portal.

This exported file can be now shared with the SOC teams,attached to audit documentation or stored as proof of endpoint security posture at a specific time.

Now that the Defender antivirus report is exported,the final step is learning how to read this data correctly.

Start on the left with the device name and device state.

This confirms which endpoint the record belongs to and whether defender currently reports the devices as clean or in another state.

Next,look at the manage by and anti-malware version.

This tells us the device is managed by Intune and confirms the Defender engine version running on the endpoint.

Useful when validating consistency across devices.

Now move to critical failure and product status.

A value of false for critical failures indicate defender is operating normally.

Product status provides a numeric representation of defender's internal health state which SOC teams use to confirm protection is active.

Scroll further and review temper protection enabled and real protection enabled.

These are key security controls.

If either of these were false,it would immediately indicate weakened endpoint protection.

Now pay attention to last reported date time and scan related timestamps.

These fields tell you when the device last reported security data.

Stale timestamps are one of the fastest ways to spot devices that appears healthy but haven't checked in recently.

Finally,scroll to the far right to see UPN,user email and user name.

This is the user context associated with the device at report time.

In defender reports,user data is secondary.

The device is always the primary security object.

The key takeaway here is this:this CSP does not show the defender exists,it proves how well it's functioning on which devices and when the status was last confirmed.

Once you can read this report confidently,you can use it for SOC reviews,audits,and incident investigations.

In this lab,you learn how to turn Intune data into real,defensible evidence.

You navigated Intune's reporting areas and generated reports across device compliance,configuration policy deployment,and endpoint security with the Microsoft Defender.

More importantly,you did not just export reports,you learned how to read them correctly.

You saw how compliance reports answer whether devices meet requirements,configuration reports prove that policies were actually delivered,and defender reports show real security posture at the device level.

By reviewing exported CSV files,you learn how to identify healthy versus risky devices,stale or non-reporting endpoints,and security controls that are enabled,disabled,or pending action.

This is the difference between simply managing Intune and operating it professionally.

Strong Intune administrators don't rely on dashboards alone.

They can produce evidence for audits,support SOC investigations,and explain security posture to leadership using data.

This completes the reporting lab and bring this section to a close.
